\mathcal{G}^{{\rm small},i}_{\rm sep}
as in (
3.24
).

Let
x\in G\cap\overline{B^{1}}
for some
G\in\mathcal{G}^{{\rm small},2}_{\rm sep}
. By definition we know that
G={\rm sat}(G)\in\mathcal{G}^{2}_{\rm sep}(v)
for some
v\in\mathcal{\tilde{V}}_{\rm sep}(B^{2})
, i.e.,
G
is a connected component of
\overline{B^{2}}\setminus\{v\}
with
|G|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
. Note that, as
B^{1}\subset B^{2}
, the set
G\cap\overline{B^{1}}
possibly consists of different connected components of
\overline{B^{1}}\setminus\{v\}
, i.e.,
G\cap\overline{B^{1}}=\bigcup_{j}G_{j}
for suitable components
(G_{j})_{j}
.

If
v\in\mathcal{\tilde{V}}_{\rm sep}(B^{1})
, i.e.,
v
is also a separating vertex of
B^{1}
, we can conclude by definition that
G_{j}\in\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\rm sep}
for all
j
. As
|{\rm sat}({G}_{j})|\leq|{\rm sat}(G\cap B^{1})|\leq|G|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
, we get
{G}_{j}\in\mathcal{G}^{{\rm small},1}_{\rm sep}
for all
j
. Since
G\cap\overline{B^{1}}=\bigcup_{j}{G}_{j}
, we have
x\in{G}_{j}
for some
j
.

If instead
v\notin\mathcal{\tilde{V}}_{\rm sep}(B^{1})
, the connected components of
\overline{B^{1}}
intersected with
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{v\}
are exactly the components of
\overline{B^{1}}\setminus\{v\}
, i.e.,
G_{j}
as above are components of
\overline{B^{1}}
intersected with
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{v\}
.
Hence, by setting accordingly
\tilde{G}_{j}=G_{j}
or
\tilde{G}_{j}=G_{j}\cup\{v\}
, for all
j
we find a set
\tilde{G}_{j}\in\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\rm sep}(\emptyset)\subset\mathcal{G}_{\rm sep}^{1}
.
Since
|G\cup\{v\}|=|G|
, we have in particular that
|{\rm sat}(\tilde{G}_{j})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\leq|G|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
and thus
\tilde{G}_{j}\in\mathcal{G}^{{\rm small},1}_{\rm sep}
for all
j
. As
G\cap\overline{B^{1}}=\bigcup_{j}\tilde{G}_{j}\setminus\{v\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we have
x\in G_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for some
j
, which concludes the proof of (
3.38
).

Modification 3
: Note that
\widehat{B}_{\rm sep}^{1}\subset\widehat{B}^{2}_{\rm sep}
is in principle not true as for a component
D\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{D}^{\rm small}_{2}(B^{2}_{\rm sep})
we might have
A_{\rm mod}^{1}\cap D\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
. However, because
D\in\mathcal{D}^{\rm small}_{2}(B^{2}_{\rm sep})
we know that
\overline{D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1}}
touches
\overline{B^{1}_{\rm sep}}\setminus(D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1})
at most at two points. Since
D={\rm sat}(D)
, we further get
|{\rm sat}(D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1})|\leq|D|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\eta^{2}}
. In particular,
{\rm sat}(D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1})=D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1}
due to the construction of
B^{1}
, and the set
D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1}
(or, respectively, all its connected components) fulfill the assumptions of Lemma
3.4
. This means we could have healed
D\cap B_{\rm sep}^{1}
already in the construction of
\widehat{B}^{1}_{\rm sep}
, which then ensures
\widehat{B}_{\rm sep}^{1}\subset\widehat{B}^{2}_{\rm sep}
. Since the healing of triangles as in (
3.33
) also preserves the monotonicity, we have
A^{1}_{\rm mod}\subset A^{2}_{\rm mod}
.
∎

3.3.
Proof of healing lemmas

It now remains to prove Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

For each
T\in\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{0}(H)\cup\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{1}(H)
, let
N^{*}_{T}
be the union of triangles in
\Omega\setminus{H}
having nonempty intersection with
T
. Then, let
N_{T}
be the connected component of
N^{*}_{T}
containing the vertex in the definition (
3.11
). Moreover, let
T^{1}
and
T^{2}
be two adjacent triangles to
T
(i.e., sharing an edge with
T
) that are contained in
N_{T}
. (Notice that the choice is unique for
\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{1}(H)
, and that there are up to three different choices of pairs
\{T^{1},T^{2}\}
for
\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{0}(H)
.) For every
u
affine on any triangle
T
, we denote by
e(u)_{T}
and
(\nabla u)_{T}
the constant matrices
e(u)
and
\nabla u
on
T
, respectively.

By Korn’s inequality we get a function
z(x)=u(x)-Ax
, where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm skew}
, such that

(3.39)

\|\nabla z\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N_{T})}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{N_{T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N_{T})}\,.

We notice that the Korn constant
K_{N_{T}}
corresponding to
N_{T}
depends only on the parameter
\theta_{0}
associated to the family of admissible triangulations.
Defining

A^{j}:=(\nabla u)_{T^{j}}-e(u)_{T^{j}}

for
j=1,2
, we deduce

(3.40)

|A-A^{j}|^{2}\,|T^{j}|=\|A-A^{j}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T^{j})}\leq 2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla z\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T^{j})}+2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T^{j})}\leq 2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(K_{N_{T}}+1)\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N_{T})},

by the identity
\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T^{j})}=|T^{j}||e(u)_{T^{j}}|^{2}
, the triangle inequality, and (
3.39
).
Denoting by
l_{j}
the unit vectors parallel to the edge in common between
T
and
T^{j}
for
j=1,2
, we get

\begin{split}\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T)}&=|T||e(u)_{T}|^{2}=|T||e(z)_{T}|^{2}\leq|T||(\nabla z)_{T}|^{2}\leq\bar{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|T|\Big(|(\nabla z)_{T}\cdot l_{1}|^{2}+|(\nabla z)_{T}\cdot l_{2}|^{2}\Big)\\
&=\bar{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|T|\Big(|(\nabla z)_{T^{1}}\cdot l_{1}|^{2}+|(\nabla z)_{T^{2}}\cdot l_{2}|^{2}\Big)\end{split}

for
\bar{C}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
depending only on
\theta_{0}
, where we used that by the continuity of
z
it holds
(\nabla z)_{T}\cdot l_{j}=(\nabla z)_{T^{j}}\cdot l_{j}
for
j=1,2
. Since
(\nabla z)_{T^{j}}=(\nabla u)_{T^{j}}-A=e(u)_{T^{j}}+A^{j}-A
, we hence obtain by (
3.40
)

\displaystyle\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|T|\Big(|(\nabla z)_{T^{1}}\cdot l_{1}|^{2}+|(\nabla z)_{T^{2}}\cdot l_{2}|^{2}\Big)\leq\bar{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|T|\sum_{j=1,2}\Big(|e(u)_{T^{j}}|^{2}+|A-A^{j}|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{j=1,2}\frac{|T|}{|T^{j}|}\Big(\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T_{j})}+2(K_{N_{T}}+1)\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N_{T})}\Big)\leq\bar{C}^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N_{T})},

where
\bar{C}^{\prime}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
just depends on
\theta_{0}
, recalling that both
K_{N_{T}}
and the volume ratio between adjacent triangles depend only on
\theta_{0}
.
We conclude by summing over
T\in\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{0}(H)\cup\mathbf{M}^{\rm heal}_{1}(H)
, observing that each triangle in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega\setminus H\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
could belong at most to a bounded number (depending on
\theta_{0}
) of different
N_{T}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Along the proof we denote by
N
,
\mathbf{T}_{N}
the sets
N_{Z}
,
\mathbf{T}_{N_{Z}}
and by
C
a universal positive constant, possibly varying from line to line, depending only on the parameter of the triangulation
\theta_{0}
. Moreover,
C_{\eta}
denotes a generic constant of the form
C\eta^{-\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
.

We first show that

(3.41)

\#\mathbf{T}_{N}\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{2}},\quad\quad|T|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}\ \text{for all $T\in\mathbf{T}_{N}$}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Indeed, recall that each edge in
\mathbf{T}
has at least length
\varepsilon
, so that we obtain
\varepsilon\#\mathcal{V}(Z)\leq\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial Z)
. Using
|Z|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
, by repeating the calculation in (
3.25
), we get
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial Z)\leq C\varepsilon/\eta^{2}
, and thus
\#\mathcal{V}(Z)\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial Z)\leq C/\eta^{2}
. Since each vertex in
\mathcal{V}(Z)
is contained in only a bounded number of triangles in
\mathbf{T}_{N}
(depending on
\theta_{0}
), we obtain the estimate
\#\mathbf{T}_{N}\leq C/\eta^{2}
. Each
T\in\mathbf{T}
,
T\subset Z
, satisfies
|T|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
. Thus, as the area of adjacent triangles is comparable by a constant depending on
\theta_{0}
, we conclude the second part of (
3.41
).

Since
Z
has no holes, we
can suppose (up to enlarging
Y
) that

N\setminus Y
consists of two connected components
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
whose closures contain the two touching points
p,\,q\in\overline{Y}\cap\overline{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see Figure
4
). In fact, if
N\setminus Y
had further components, their closure would only intersect with
Z
at one of the touching points and not share an edge with a triangle in
\overline{Z}
.
We now claim that
there are
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1},A_{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm skew}
such that

(3.42)

\|\nabla u-A_{j}\|_{L^{2}(T)}\leq C_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})}\quad\quad\text{for \color[rgb]{0,0,0}each triangle \color[rgb]{0,0,0}}T\subset\overline{N}_{j},\,\;j=1,2.

To see this, for every
T\subset\overline{N}_{j}
, let

(3.43)

(\nabla u)_{T}=e(u)_{T}+A_{T},

for
(\nabla u)_{T}
,
e(u)_{T}
, and
A_{T}
suitable matrices representing the constant values of
\nabla u
,
e(u)
, and
\nabla u-e(u)
on
T
.
Given two adjacent triangles
T_{1},T_{2}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}_{N}
in
N_{j}
, i.e., sharing an edge, let us consider the circle
C_{1,2}
with the maximal radius among those circles centered on a point of the common edge and included in
T_{1}\cup T_{2}
. The radius of any
C_{1,2}
is larger than
\varepsilon/\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C
, where
C
depends only on
\theta_{0}
. Applying Korn’s inequality on
C_{1,2}
, we find
A_{1,2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm skew}
and
K>0
(the Korn constant for a circle) such that

\|\nabla u-A_{1,2}\|_{L^{2}(C_{1,2})}\leq K\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(C_{1,2})},

and then

\|A_{T_{j}}-A_{1,2}\|_{L^{2}(C_{1,2}\cap T_{j})}\leq(K+1)\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(C_{1,2})}.

Since
A_{T_{j}}
are constant matrices, noticing that
\|A_{T_{j}}-A_{1,2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(C_{1,2}\cap T_{j})}=\frac{|C_{1,2}|}{2}|A_{T_{j}}-A_{1,2}|^{2}
, we deduce by the triangle inequality that

(3.44)

|A_{T_{1}}-A_{T_{2}}|^{2}\leq\frac{8}{|C_{1,2}|}(K+1)^{2}\|e(u)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(C_{1,2})}.

Being
N_{j}
connected, given any
\tilde{T}_{1}
,
\tilde{T}_{2}\subset\overline{N}_{j}
, there are triangles
\tilde{\mathbf{T}}:=(\hat{T}_{j})_{j=1}^{n}\subset\mathbf{T}
included in
\overline{N_{j}}
with
\hat{T}_{1}=\tilde{T}_{1}
,
\hat{T}_{n}=\tilde{T}_{2}
, such that
\hat{T}_{j}
,
\hat{T}_{j+1}
are adjacent for all
j=1,\ldots,n-1
. We apply the estimate (
3.44
) for pairs of adjacent triangles a finite number of times (less than
\#\mathbf{T}_{N}
). Then, using the triangle inequality and also noting that the sets
C_{1,2}
are such that
|C_{1,2}|\geq\varepsilon^{2}/C
and that circles corresponding to different pairs overlap at most twice,
we find that

(3.45)

|A_{\tilde{T}_{1}}-A_{\tilde{T}_{2}}|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\#\mathbf{T}_{N}}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})}\leq\frac{C_{\eta}}{\varepsilon}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})},

where in the last step we used (
3.41
). We now confirm (
3.42
) by fixing
A_{j}
as one of the
A_{T}
,
T\subset\overline{N_{j}}
. Notice that for every
T\subset\overline{N}_{j}
, recalling the notation (
3.43
), we indeed have by (
3.41
) and (
3.45
)

\|\nabla u-A_{j}\|_{L^{2}(T)}\leq\|\nabla u-A_{T}\|_{L^{2}(T)}+\|A_{j}-A_{T}\|_{L^{2}(T)}=\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(T)}+\|A_{j}-A_{T}\|_{L^{2}(T)}\leq C_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})}.

This concludes the proof of (
3.42
).

By the fact that
u
is affine on each
T\in\mathbf{T}_{N}
,
|T|\geq\varepsilon^{2}/C
, and by (
3.42
) it also follows that

(3.46)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|(\nabla u)_{T}-A_{j}|=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla u-A_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(T)}\leq\frac{C_{\eta}}{\varepsilon}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})}\quad\quad\text{for}\;j=1,2.

The fundamental point in the proof is now that

(3.47)

|A_{2}-A_{1}|\leq\frac{C_{\eta}}{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N\setminus Y)}.

In fact, recall that
p
and
q
are connected by a path consisting of less than
\#\mathbf{T}_{N}
edges of triangles in
\mathbf{T}_{N}
. Then, by (
3.46
) and the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus applied on any edge of the path connecting
p
and
q
to the scalar-valued functions
(u-A_{j}\cdot)_{i}
,
j=1,2
,
i=1,2
(here,
(u-A_{j}\cdot)_{i}
are the two components of
x\mapsto u(x)-A_{j}x
for
j=1,2
), we get

(3.48)

\big|\big(u(q)-u(p)-A_{j}(q-p)\big)_{i}\big|\leq C_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N_{j})},

where we used that the path of edges has a length of order
\sim\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta^{2}}
, because
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial Z)\leq C\varepsilon/\eta^{2}
, see below (
3.41
). Subtracting the two terms in (
3.48
) for
j=1,2
and for fixed
i
, we get

|\big((A_{2}-A_{1})(q-p)\big)_{i}|\leq C_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N\setminus Y)},

which confirms (
3.47
) since
A_{j}
are skew symmetric and
|q-p|\in[\varepsilon,C\eta^{-2}\varepsilon]
.

Combining (
3.46
) and (
3.47
) we deduce that

(3.49)

\|\nabla u-A_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(N\setminus Y)}\leq\frac{C_{\eta}}{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N\setminus Y)}.

By McShane’s theorem we find a Lipschitz extension
\widetilde{w}
of
u-A_{1}\cdot
from
N\setminus\overline{Y}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
to
{\rm int}(\overline{Z\cup(N\setminus Y)})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
whose components have the same Lipschitz constant as
u-A_{1}\cdot
. In particular,

(3.50)

\|\nabla\widetilde{w}\|_{L^{\infty}((N\setminus Y)\cup Z)}\leq\frac{C_{\eta}}{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N\setminus Y)},\qquad\widetilde{w}=u-A_{1}\cdot\text{ on }N\setminus\overline{Y}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We set

u_{\rm heal}:=\widetilde{w}+A_{1}\cdot.

The second condition in (
3.50
) immediately gives that
u_{\rm heal}=u
on
N\setminus\overline{Y}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Moreover,

\begin{split}\|e(u_{\rm heal})\|_{L^{2}((N\setminus Y)\cup Z)}&=\|e(\widetilde{w})\|_{L^{2}((N\setminus Y)\cup Z)}\leq\|\nabla\widetilde{w}\|_{L^{2}((N\setminus Y)\cup Z)}\\
&\leq|(N\setminus Y)\cup Z|^{1/2}\|\nabla\widetilde{w}\|_{L^{\infty}((N\setminus Y)\cup Z)}\leq C_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(u)\|_{L^{2}(N\setminus Y)},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used (
3.50
) and the fact that

|Z|\leq\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
by
assumption as well as that
|N|
is controlled by
C\varepsilon^{2}/\eta^{4}
due to (
3.41
), respectively. This concludes the proof.
∎

4.
Approximation of quasi-static crack growth

This section is devoted to the formulation of our main result. We present a convergence result for an evolutionary problem with respect to the adaptive finite-element approximation introduced in Section
2
. More precisely, we set up a time-incremental minimization scheme and prove the convergence to a continuum quasi-static crack growth in the spirit of
Francfort and Larsen

[
26
]
. In particular, we recover the existence result of a fracture evolution in linearized elasticity
[
33
]
. The main issue compared to the
\Gamma
-convergence result in Section
2.2
consists in dealing with the irreversibility of the fracture process.

4.1.
Quasi-static adaptive finite element model

We define an arbitrary sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\subset(0,\infty)
with
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Instead of considering general densities
f
with properties (
2.2
), we consider for simplicity only the special case
f(t)=t\wedge\kappa
and
\mathbb{C}=\mathrm{Id}_{2{\times}2\times 2\times 2}
. The case of general
\mathbb{C}
can be treated in the same way, adjusting the notation accordingly. Moreover, we assume that
the function
\omega\colon\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is given by

(4.1)

\displaystyle\omega(\varepsilon_{n})=10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}.

The constant
10^{6}
is chosen for definiteness only. This assumption could be removed at the expense of additional estimates which we omit for simplicity.

In order to introduce boundary conditions on a part
\partial_{D}\Omega\subset\partial\Omega
of the boundary, we impose boundary conditions in a
neighborhood
of the boundary. More precisely, we suppose that there exists another Lipschitz set
{\Omega^{\prime}}\supset\Omega
with
\partial_{D}\Omega=\partial\Omega\cap{\Omega^{\prime}}
such that also
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
is Lipschitz. For a given boundary datum
g\in W^{2,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and a triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
, we define

(4.2)

g_{{\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}
as the piecewise affine interpolation of
g
on
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Recalling (
2.4
), we then consider the energy

(4.3)

\displaystyle E_{n}(u)\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}|e(u)|^{2}\wedge\frac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,{\rm d}x

if
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
and if for the (possibly non uniquely chosen) triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)\in\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
(see (
2.1
)) it holds
u=g_{\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)}
on each triangle
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)
such that
T\cap\overline{\Omega}=\emptyset
. Otherwise, we set
E_{n}(u)=+\infty
. We emphasize that the energy is still defined as an integral over
\Omega
although the functions
u
are defined on the larger set
\Omega^{\prime}
.

Now we introduce a time discrete evolution which is driven by time-dependent boundary conditions
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))
. Given a sequence
(\delta_{n})_{n}\subset(0,\infty)
with
\delta_{n}\to 0
and for each
\delta_{n}
, we consider the subdivision
0=t^{0}_{n}<\dots<t_{n}^{T/\delta_{n}}=T
of the interval
[0,T]
with step size
\delta_{n}
. (Without restriction, we assume that
T/\delta_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
.) Correspondingly, let
(g(t^{k}_{n}))_{k}
be the sequence of boundary data at different time steps
k\in\{0,\dots,T/\delta_{n}\}
.

We assume for the moment that a displacement history
(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
at time steps
(t^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
is given, and introduce admissible competitors and the energy for the next time step, taking into account the irreversibility of the process.

Consider
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
and the corresponding triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u)
. Recall the definition of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\rm big}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u)\colon\varepsilon_{n}|e(u)_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa\}
and the definition of
\Omega^{\rm big}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(u)
in (
2.7
). In place of
\Omega^{\rm big}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(u)
, we now define a possibly larger ‘crack set’
\Omega_{n}^{\rm crack}(u)
by considering also triangles that are very far away from a regular background mesh. More precisely, let
\mathbf{Z}_{n}
be the triangulation that is based on the square grid of size
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}\coloneqq 2\varepsilon_{n}\cos(\theta_{0})
with nodes contained in
\varepsilon_{n}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{2}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
, where each square is then cut into two triangles along the diagonal (see
[
14
, Figure 5.11]
). For a function
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
with triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u)
, we define
\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u)\coloneqq\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)\cap\mathbf{Z}_{n}
as the part of the triangulation that belongs to this regular background mesh and let
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u))\coloneqq\min\{\dist(T,\tilde{T})\colon\tilde{T}\in\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u)\}
. We then define

(4.4)

\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u)\coloneqq\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)\colon\varepsilon_{n}|e(u)_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa\quad\text{or}\quad\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u))\geq 10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}\big\}.

The associated crack set is then defined as the union of all such triangles in
\Omega^{\prime}
, this means

(4.5)

\Omega_{n}^{\rm crack}(u)\coloneqq\mathrm{int}\Big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)}T\Big)\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Note that, additionally to the condition on the gradient, we also regard triangles as ‘cracked’ if they are far away from a fixed background mesh. This means in particular that, if
\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u)=\emptyset
, we would have
\Omega_{n}^{\rm crack}(u)=\Omega^{\prime}
. This condition is inspired by the construction of recovery sequences in
[
14
, Appendix]
where all triangles are close to a background mesh, i.e., in that situation the additional condition is not active. In our evolutionary setting, we expect the same, and thus the condition is merely of technical nature. Let us also emphasize that the constant
10^{6}
is chosen for definiteness only and could be chosen arbitrarily large, but fixed. Both requirements in (
4.4
) will turn out to be crucial for our proof of the stability of the static equilibrium property, see Theorem
6.7
below.

Given a displacement history
(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
, we define

(4.6)

\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j<k}\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{j}_{n})\quad\quad\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j<k}\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{j}_{n})\,.

For a given displacement
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
we also set

\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u)\coloneqq\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u)\quad\quad\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u)\coloneqq\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\cup\Omega_{n}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u)\,.

Similar to the splitting in (
2.8
), we define the corresponding history-dependent energy by

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}(u,(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}):=\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|e(u)|^{2}\,+\,\kappa\,\frac{|\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|}{\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}^{\rm elast}_{n}(u,(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k})+\mathcal{E}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u,(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}).

Note that the set
\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}
and thus the energy take the ‘cracked triangles’ of all previous time steps into account. In general, the triangulations at each time could be different and without additional requirements it is not guaranteed that the union
\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}
is consistent with an admissible triangulation. In particular,
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}
is not necessarily a triangulation partitioning
\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}
. For this reason, we introduce a further restriction as we set up the time-incremental minimization scheme. More precisely, recalling (
2.1
), we set
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{0}(\Omega^{\prime}):=\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
and for
k\geq 1
we introduce the set
\hat{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{n}(\Omega^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
that depends on the displacement history
(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
and consists of all functions
u\colon\Omega^{\prime}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that there exists a triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})
with
u
being piecewise affine on
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and such that
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
fulfills

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We then define

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}\coloneqq\big\{v\in\hat{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{n}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\text{and}\;v=g(t^{k}_{n})_{\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(v)}\;\text{on all $T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(v)$ with }\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T\cap\overline{\Omega}=\emptyset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}.

Inductively, provided that
u^{j}_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{j}_{n}
for
0\leq j\leq k-1
, we see that
\hat{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{n}(\Omega^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
and then also
{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{n}\neq\emptyset
for all
k\geq 1
since the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{k-1}_{n})
satisfies
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{k-1}_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

We suppose that the
initial value

u_{n}^{0}\in\mathcal{A}^{0}_{n}
is a minimum configuration in the sense that

(4.10)

u_{n}^{0}\in{\rm argmin}\big\{E_{n}(v)\colon v\in\mathcal{A}^{0}_{n}\big\}\,,

with
E_{n}
given as in (
4.3
). We inductively define an evolution as follows: given
(u_{n}^{j})_{0\leq j\leq k-1}
, we let

(4.11)

u_{n}^{k}\in\argmin\big\{\mathcal{E}_{n}(v,(u_{n}^{j})_{j<k})\,\colon v\in\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}\big\},

i.e., the minimization problem involves the previous time steps, according to the definition in (
4.7
). The existence of minimizers in (
4.11
) immediately follows from the direct method since
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}\neq\emptyset
, the problem is finite dimensional for a fixed triangulation,
\mathcal{E}_{n}(\cdot,(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k})
is continuous, and the set of admissible interpolations
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}
is compact.

4.2.
Quasi-static fracture evolution

We consider the
Griffith energy

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(u,K):=\int_{\Omega}|e(u)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\kappa\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sin(\theta_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(K)\,,

for each
u\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and each rectifiable set
K\subset\Omega\cup\partial_{D}\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(K)<+\infty
, where
e(u)
denotes the approximate symmetric gradient and
J_{u}
is the jump set of
u
, which is subject to the constraint
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{u}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
. (Here and in the following,
\,\tilde{\subset}\,
stands for inclusions up to
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible sets.) We highlight that, although the elastic energy is defined on
\Omega
, the functions are defined on the larger set
\Omega^{\prime}
and the crack sets
K
may intersect the Dirichlet boundary
\partial_{D}\Omega
.

By
AD(g,H)
we denote all functions
v\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that

(4.13)

v=g\text{ on }\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega},\quad J_{v}\ \tilde{\subset}\ H.

Definition 4.1
.

We define an
irreversible quasi-static crack evolution
with respect to the boundary condition
g\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
as any mapping
t\to(u(t),\Gamma(t))
with
u(t)\in AD(g(t),\Gamma(t))
for all
t\in[0,T]
such that the following four conditions hold:

(a)

Initial condition
:
u(0)
minimizes
\mathcal{E}(u,J_{u})
given in (
4.12
) among all
v\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
with

v=g(0)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
.

(b)

Irreversibility
:
\Gamma(t_{1})\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Gamma(t_{2})
for all
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T
.

(c)

Global stability
: For every
t\in(0,T]
, for every
H
with
\Gamma(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,H
, and for every
v\in AD(g(t),H)
it holds that

(4.14)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}(u(t),\Gamma(t))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\mathcal{E}(v,H)\,.

(d)

Energy balance
: The function
t\mapsto\mathcal{E}(u(t),\Gamma(t))
is absolutely continuous and it holds that

(4.15)

\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{E}(u(t),\Gamma(t))=\int_{\Omega}e(u(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):\nabla\partial_{t}g(t)\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{for a.e.\ $t\in[0,T]$}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where by
\partial_{t}
we denote the time derivative of
g
.

In
[
33
]
(see
[
26
]
for the scalar case), the existence of an irreversible quasi-static crack evolution with respect to the boundary displacement
g
has been shown. In this present work, our goal consists in approximating such an evolution with the time-discretized evolution defined in Section
4.1
.

4.3.
Main result: Approximation of quasi-static crack growth

To formulate our main result, we need to introduce some further notation. First, we associate a ‘crack surface’ to the finite element evolution
(u_{n}^{k})_{k}
. To this end,
we choose
\eta_{n}\to 0
depending on
\varepsilon_{n}
such that

(4.16)

(C_{\eta_{n}})^{7}\varepsilon_{n}\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

where
C_{\eta_{n}}\geq 1
denotes the constant in Theorem
2.1
, and
we apply Theorem
2.1
to
\eta_{n}
,
u_{n}^{k}
, and
A=\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
. (Here, we consider
\Omega^{\prime}
as the ambient space in place of
\Omega
, in particular we replace
\Omega
with
\Omega^{\prime}
in (
2.10
), see also (
4.5
).)
We obtain a set
\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k}
and a function
u^{\rm mod}_{n,k}
satisfying

(4.17)

\begin{split}&|\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k}|\leq C_{\eta_{n}}\varepsilon_{n},\qquad|\{u_{n}^{k}\neq u^{\rm mod}_{n,k}\}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n},\eta_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\leq C_{\eta_{n}}\varepsilon_{n},\quad\\
\|e(u^{\rm mod}_{n,k})&\|_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n},\eta_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k})}\leq C_{\eta_{n}},\quad\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}\sin\theta_{0}}|\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|+C\eta_{n},\end{split}

where the constants
C
and
C_{\eta_{n}}
also depend on
\max_{0\leq k\leq T/\delta_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}^{k},(u_{n}^{j})_{j<k})
.

We define the evolution
u_{n}\colon[0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
, piecewise affine in space and piecewise constant in time, by

(4.18)

u_{n}(t):=u^{k}_{n}\chi_{\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\;\text{for}\;t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k+1}_{n})\,.

The crack set
K_{n}
is defined by

(4.19)

K_{n}(t)\coloneqq\partial{\Omega}^{\rm mod}_{n,k}\quad\text{for $t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k+1}_{n})$}\,.

For the crack sets, we use the notion of
\sigma
-convergence
recalled in Definition
5.1
below, which is a suitable notion of convergence for crack sets. In particular, below we will obtain the existence of
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\Omega}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
for
t\in[0,T]
such that
K_{n}(t)

\sigma
-converges to
K(t)
for
t\in[0,T]
.

As a final preparation, we identify sets on which convergence of displacement fields can be guaranteed. For a crack set
\Gamma(t)\subset\overline{\Omega}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})<\infty
, by
B(t)\subset\Omega
we denote the largest set of finite perimeter (with respect to set inclusion) which satisfies
\partial^{*}B(t)\cap\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Gamma(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. This set represents the ‘broken off pieces’, and by
G(t):=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus B(t)
instead we denote the ‘good set’, which in particular satisfies
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}\subset G(t)
. Note that convergence of the displacements can only be expected on
G(t)
, see
[
35
, Subsection 2.4]
for a thorough discussion. Moreover, we note that for an evolution
t\mapsto(u(t),\Gamma(t))
in the sense of Definition
4.1
it holds that

(4.20)

e(u(t))=0\quad\text{ on }B(t)\quad\text{ for all $t\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[0,T]$}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In fact, this follows by applying (
4.14
) with test set
H=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and test function
v=u(t)\chi_{G(t)}
.

The main result of this paper is the following convergence theorem.

Theorem 4.2

(Approximation of quasi-static crack growth)
.

There exists a quasi-static crack evolution
t\to(u(t),K(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with respect to the boundary condition
g
such that, up to a subsequence, we have that

(4.21)

\displaystyle K_{n}(t)\ \sigma\text{-converges to }K(t)\text{ for all }t\in[0,T]

as
n\to\infty
,
K(0)=J_{u(0)}
, and that, for each
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\text{ $\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u_{n}(t)\to u(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$ as $k\to\infty$ in measure on $G(t)$,}\quad e(u_{n}(t))\to e(u(t))\text{ in }L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym}),

where
G(t)
is the set corresponding to
K(t)
defined before (
4.20
). Moreover, for all
t\in[0,T]
we have that

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t),(u_{n}^{j})_{j<k(t)})\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}(u(t),K(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$},

where for each
n
the (
n
-dependent) index
k(t)\in\mathbb{N}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is chosen such that
t\in[t^{k(t)}_{n},t^{k(t)+1}_{n})
.

Remark 4.3
.

We proceed with two comments on the result:

(i)

The energy convergence (
4.23
) can be improved to separate energy convergence in the sense that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathcal{E}^{\rm elast}_{n}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u_{n}(t),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k(t)}\big)\to\int_{\Omega}|e(u(t))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(recall (
4.7
)) and

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{\rm crack}_{n}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u_{n}(t),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k(t)}\big)=\frac{\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n}(t))=\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(K(t))\,.

(ii)

The identity (
4.24
) is the reason why crack sets along the sequence are defined in terms of
\partial{\Omega}^{\rm mod}_{n,k}
and not in terms of
\partial\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
. In fact, for the latter the identity (
4.24
) does not hold in general.

5.
Preparations

In this section, we collect some tools and remarks that we will need to prove Theorem
4.2
in the next section. Before providing the necessary compactness and semicontinuity statements, we recall a suitable notion of convergence for crack sets and prove, respectively recall, the necessary compactness and irreversiblity results.

5.1.
Convergence of sets

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
for
d\geq 2
and denote by
e_{1}
the first unit vector. We let

(5.1)

{\rm PC}(\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):=\{v\in L^{1}(\Omega;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d})\colon v=e_{1}\chi_{T}\text{ with }T\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{ of finite perimeter}\}

be the collection of piecewise constant functions taking values in
\{0,e_{1}\}
. In this subsection, by
\mathcal{A}(\Omega)
we denote the family of open subsets of
\Omega
. We recall the notion of
\sigma
-convergence from
[
40
]
.

Definition 5.1
.

[
40
, Definition 5.1]

A sequence of rectifiable sets
(K_{n})_{n}
in
\Omega

\sigma
-converges in
\Omega
to
K
if the functionals
\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}\colon{\rm PC}(\Omega){\times}\mathcal{A}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty)
defined by

(5.2)

\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}(u,A):=\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big((J_{u}\setminus K_{n})\cap A)

are such that, for every
A\in\mathcal{A}(\Omega)
,
\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges with respect to the strong topology of
L^{1}(\Omega)
to
\mathcal{H}^{-}(\cdot,A)
, where
\mathcal{H}^{-}\colon{\rm PC}(\Omega){\times}\mathcal{A}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty)
is given by

(5.3)

\mathcal{H}^{-}(u,A):=\int_{J_{u}\cap A}h^{-}(x,\nu_{u})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x),

and
K
is the maximal (with respect to
\tilde{\subset}
) rectifiable set in
\Omega
such that

(5.4)

h^{-}(x,\nu_{K}(x))=0\quad\text{for }\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\text{-a.e.\ }x\in K.

Remark 5.2
.

(i) More precisely, if
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n})\leq C
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, by
[
40
, Proposition 3.3]
the density
h^{-}
in (
5.3
) is characterized by

(5.5)

h^{-}(x,\nu)=\limsup_{\varrho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{\mathbf{m}^{\mathrm{PC}}_{\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}}(\overline{u}_{x,\nu},Q_{\varrho}^{\nu}(x))}{\varrho^{d-1}},

for
x\in\Omega
and
\nu\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon|y|=1\}
, where
Q_{\varrho}^{\nu}(x)
denotes a suitable cube with sidelength
\varrho
and two sides orthogonal to
\nu
,
\overline{u}_{x,\nu}:=e_{1}\chi_{Q_{\varrho}^{\nu,-}(x)}
(with
Q_{\varrho}^{\nu,-}(x)=\{y\in Q_{\varrho}^{\nu}(x)\colon(y-x)\cdot\nu<0\}
), and

(5.6)

\mathbf{m}^{\mathrm{PC}}_{\mathcal{H}}(\overline{v},A):=\inf_{v\in\mathrm{PC}(A)}\{\mathcal{H}(v,A)\colon v=\overline{v}\text{ in a neighborhood of }\partial A\}\ \text{ for }\overline{v}\in\mathrm{PC}(A),\,A\in\mathcal{A}(\Omega).

In particular, using
\overline{u}_{x,\nu}
as competitor, we get
h^{-}\leq 1
.

(ii) Let
(K_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets in
\Omega
such that
K_{n}

\sigma
-converges to
K
in
\Omega
and let
(\tilde{K}_{n})_{n}
be another sequence of rectifiable sets such that
\tilde{K}_{n}

\sigma
-converges to
\tilde{K}
in
\Omega
, and
\tilde{K}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have
\tilde{K}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K
.

(iii) If
F
is a closed set such that
K_{n}\,\tilde{\subset}\,F
, then the
\sigma
-limit
K
of
(K_{n})_{n}
, satisfies
K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}F
: in fact,
\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}(u,\Omega\setminus F)=\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap(\Omega\setminus F))
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and thus
\mathcal{H}^{-}(u,\Omega\setminus F)=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap(\Omega\setminus F))
, i.e.,
h^{-}
cannot be 0 on a subset of
\Omega\setminus F
of positive
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-measure.

In the following, we need the compactness and lower semicontinuity properties of
\sigma
-convergence, see
[
40
, Propositions 5.3, proof of Theorem 8.1]
.

Proposition 5.3

(Compactness and lower semicontinuity)
.

Let
(K_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets in
\Omega
with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n})\leq C
. Then there exists a subsequence
(n_{k})_{k}
and a rectifiable set
K
in
\Omega
such that
K_{n_{k}}

\sigma
-converges in
\Omega
to
K
. Moreover,

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n})\,.

Theorem 5.4

(Variant of Helly’s theorem for
\sigma
-convergence)
.

Let
t\mapsto\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K{}_{n}(t)
be a sequence of increasing set functions defined on an interval
I\subset\mathbb{R}
with values contained in
\Omega
, i.e.,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(s)\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t)\subset\Omega
for every
s,t\in I
with
s<t
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K{}_{n}(t))
is bounded uniformly with respect to
n
and
t
. Then, there exist a subsequence
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K{}_{n_{k}})_{k}
and an increasing set function
t\mapsto\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t)
on
I
such that for every
t\in I
we have

(a)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K{}_{n_{k}}(t)\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t)
in the sense of
\sigma
-convergence,

(b)

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t))\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K{}_{n_{k}}(t))
.

For definition and properties of
SBV^{p}(\Omega)
,
p\in(1,\infty)
, we refer the reader to
[
2
]
. In the following, we say
v_{n}\rightharpoonup v
weakly in
SBV^{p}(\Omega)
if
v_{n}\to v
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
and
\sup_{n}(\|\nabla v_{n}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v_{n}}))<+\infty
. We will also make use of the following property of
\sigma
-convergence (see
[
40
, Proposition 5.8]
).

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
(K_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets in
\Omega
such that
K_{n}

\sigma
-converges to
K
in
\Omega
. Let
(v_{n})_{n}
be a sequence
SBV^{p}(\Omega)
with
v_{n}\rightharpoonup v
weakly in
SBV^{p}(\Omega)
and
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\to 0
. Then
J_{v}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K
.

We close this section with a lower semicontinuity result for the boundaries of void sets.

Lemma 5.6

(Lower semicontinuity for void sets)
.

Let
K_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets of the form
K_{n}=\partial V_{n}
for closed sets
V_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with
finite perimeter and
|V_{n}|\to 0
. Suppose that
K_{n}

\sigma
-converges to
K
. Then

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n})\geq 2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K).

Proof.

Given
\mu>0
, by a covering argument there exist an open and smooth set
U\subset\Omega
and a function
v\in{\rm PC}(\Omega)
such that

(5.7)

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K\setminus U)\leq\mu,\quad\quad\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((K\,\triangle\,J_{v})\cap U)\leq\mu\,.

Let
(v_{n})_{n}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm PC}(U)
be a recovery sequence in
L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
for the restriction of
v
to
U
with respect to the functionals (
5.2
) and (
5.3
). Since
h^{-}\leq 1
, see Remark
5.2
(i), we have

(5.8)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\cap U)\leq\int_{(J_{v}\setminus K)\cap U}h^{-}(x,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((J_{v}\setminus K)\cap U)\leq\mu\,.

We notice that the function

\tilde{v}_{n}\coloneqq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi_{U\setminus{V}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v_{n}

is such that

(5.9)

\tilde{v}_{n}\to v\quad\text{in }L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}),

since
|V_{n}|\to 0
. Notice that the function
\tilde{v}_{n}
might also jump outside of
\partial V_{n}
because
J_{v_{n}}\cap(U\setminus{V_{n}})\neq\emptyset
is in general possible. Therefore, we apply
[
7
, Proposition 9]
to the open set
\tilde{\Omega}_{n}:=U\setminus V_{n}
(in place of
\Omega
therein) to obtain a sequence of approximating pairs
((z_{h},F_{h}))_{h}
, with
z_{h}\in SBV^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{n};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
and
F_{h}
of class
C^{\infty}
, such that

J_{z_{h}}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\partial F_{h},\quad z_{h}=0\text{ in }F_{h},\quad z_{h}\to\tilde{v}_{n}=v_{n}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{ in }\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}_{n};\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad F_{h}\to\emptyset,

and

(5.10)

\limsup_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial F_{h})=2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\tilde{v}_{n}}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\cap U).

For
h_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.11)

\|z_{h_{n}}-v_{n}\|_{L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}_{n})}+|F_{h_{n}}|+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial F_{h_{n}})-2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\cap U)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{1}{n},

setting

w_{n}:=z_{h_{n}}\chi_{\tilde{\Omega}_{n}}\in SBV^{2}(U;\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad G_{n}:=F_{h_{n}}\cup(V_{n}\cap U),

it holds that

w_{n}=0\text{ in }G_{n},\quad J_{w_{n}}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\partial G_{n},\quad w_{n}\to v\quad\text{ in }L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}).

Therefore,
[
7
, Proposition 3]
gives

(5.12)

2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v}\cap U)\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial G_{n}).

By (
5.8
) and (
5.11
) it holds that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial F_{h_{n}})\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}((J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\cap U)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq 2\mu.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Since
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial G_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n}\cap U)+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial F_{h_{n}})
,
we get

(5.13)

\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n}\cap U)\geq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial G_{n})-2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu.

By (
5.7
), (
5.12
), and (
5.13
) we deduce

\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n})\geq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K_{n}\cap U)\geq 2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v}\cap U)-2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu\geq 2\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(K)-6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu,

and we conclude by the arbitrariness of
\mu>0
. ∎

5.2.
Compactness

From now on we restrict ourselves to the case
d=2
. For technical reasons, we need to consider the space of
GSBD^{p}
functions (with
p\in(1,\infty)
) which may also attain a limit value
\infty
. Following
[
17
, Subsection 4.4]
, we define
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:=\mathbb{R}^{2}\cup\{\infty\}
, and for
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
open, we let

\displaystyle GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U):=\Big\{

\displaystyle u\in L^{0}(U;\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{2})\colon\,A^{\infty}_{u}:=\{u=\infty\}\text{ satisfies }\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}A^{\infty}_{u})<+\infty,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \tilde{u}_{t}:=u\chi_{U\setminus A^{\infty}_{u}}+t\chi_{A^{\infty}_{u}}\in GSBD^{p}(U)\ \text{ for all $t\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$}\Big\}.

Symbolically, we also write

u=u\chi_{U\setminus A^{\infty}_{u}}+\infty\chi_{A^{\infty}_{u}},

and for any
u\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
, we set

(5.15)

\displaystyle e(u)=0\text{ in }A^{\infty}_{u},\quad\quad\quad J_{u}=J_{u\chi_{\Omega\setminus A^{\infty}_{u}}}\cup(\partial^{*}A^{\infty}_{u}\cap U).

We also define the subspaces
PR(U)\subset GSBD^{p}(U)
and
PR_{\infty}(U)\subset GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
as the functions in
u\in GSBD^{p}(U)
and
u\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
, respectively, with
e(u)\equiv 0
. Functions
a\in PR_{\infty}(U)
are
piecewise rigid
in the sense that they can be represented as

a=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}a^{j}\chi_{P^{j}}+\infty\chi_{A_{a}^{\infty}},

where
a^{j}
are affine mappings with
e(a^{j})=0
and
(P^{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Caccioppoli partition of
U\setminus A_{a}^{\infty}
. If
a\in PR(U)
, then
A_{a}^{\infty}=\emptyset
.

There exists a metric
\bar{d}
on
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
, see
[
17
, Equation (3.13)]
, which induces the following convergence:
\bar{d}(u_{n},u)\to 0
if and only if

u_{n}\to u\quad\text{ in measure on }U\setminus A_{u}^{\infty},\quad\quad|u_{n}|\to\infty\quad\text{ on $A_{u}^{\infty}$}.

In the following, we say that a sequence
(u_{n})_{n}\subset GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)

converges weakly
to
u\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
if

(5.16)

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\|e(u_{n})\|_{L^{p}(U)}+\mathcal{H}^{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(J_{u_{n}})\big)<+\infty

and

\text{$\bar{d}(u_{n},u)\to 0$, \quad\quad\quad$e(u_{n})\rightharpoonup e(u)$ weakly in $L^{p}(U\setminus A_{u}^{\infty};\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})$}.

Proposition 5.7

(Compactness)
.

Let
(u_{n})_{n}\subset GSBD^{p}(U)
satisfy (
5.16
). Then there exists
u\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
such that, up to a subsequence,
u_{n}
converges weakly to
u
in
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(U)
. If additionally
u_{n}\in PR_{\infty}(U)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we get
u\in PR_{\infty}(U)
.

Proof.

The result follows from
[
17
, Theorem 3.5 and Lemma 3.6]
which itself is a consequence of
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
. The closedness of
PR_{\infty}
follows by repeating the argument in
[
34
, Lemma 3.3]
.
∎

For the next result, we again consider Lipschitz sets
\Omega\subset\Omega^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
is Lipschitz, as considered in Section
4
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
(K_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets in
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
, with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n})\leq C
which
\sigma
-converges to
K\subset\Omega^{\prime}
. Let
(v_{n})_{n}
be a sequence converging weakly in
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
to
v\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
and
v_{n}|_{\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}}
is a bounded sequence in
W^{1,p}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then the following hold:

(i)

J_{v}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
.

(ii)

It holds that
|A^{\infty}_{v}\cap G_{K}|=0
, where
G_{K}
denotes the smallest set of finite perimeter with
|\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(\overline{\Omega}\cup G_{K})|=0
and
\partial^{*}G_{K}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
.

Later we will apply this proposition to sequences satisfying Dirichlet boundary conditions which guarantees the boundedness assumption on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
. The set
G(t)
introduced before (
4.20
) will play the role of
G_{K}
. The result ensures the compatibility of the notion of
\sigma
-convergence with the weak notion of
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}
-convergence for the displacement fields, and can be seen as the
GSBD
-analog of
[
40
, Proposition 5.8]
. More precisely, it enables us to show that limiting evolutions
t\mapsto u(t)
fulfill
J_{u(t)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(t)
and are thus admissible with respect to
t\mapsto K(t)
. Before we come to the proof, we show the following preliminary result.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
(K_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of rectifiable sets in
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
, with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n})\leq C
which
\sigma
-converges to
K\subset\Omega^{\prime}
. Consider the functionals

(5.17)

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}(u):=\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|e(u)|^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{u}\setminus K_{n})

if
u\in GSBD^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
and
+\infty
otherwise in
L^{0}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}(u)

\Gamma
-converges, with respect to the convergence in measure in
\Omega^{\prime}
, to the functional

(5.18)

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(u):=\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|e(u)|^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{u}}h^{-}(x,\nu_{u})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)

if
u\in GSBD^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
and
+\infty
otherwise in
L^{0}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, where
h^{-}
is the density given in Remark
5.2
(i) for
d=2
.

Proof of Lemma
5.9
.

Our strategy is to apply the abstract
\Gamma
-convergence result in
[
31
]
to the sequence
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}
. As a pointwise lower bound on the density of the surface integral is needed, cf.
[
31
, Assumption (2.12)]
, we consider a suitable perturbation.
Precisely, given
\varepsilon>0
, let us define the functionals
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}(u):=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}(u)+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{u}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{n})
, i.e.,

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}(u)=\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|e(u)|^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{u}\setminus K_{n})+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{u}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{n})

if
u\in GSBD^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
and
+\infty
otherwise in
L^{0}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The characterization of the
\Gamma
-limit of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}
with respect to the convergence in measure follows from
[
31
, Theorem 2.4, Remark 3.15]
:
the
\Gamma
-limit of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}
is

(5.19)

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(u):=\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|e(u)|^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{u}}h_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x,\nu_{u}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x),

if
u\in GSBD^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
and
+\infty
otherwise in
L^{0}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
with

(5.20)

h_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x,\nu)=\limsup_{\varrho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{\mathbf{m}^{\mathrm{PC}}_{\mathcal{H}_{n,\varepsilon}^{-}}(\overline{u}_{x,\nu},Q_{\varrho}^{\nu}(x))}{\varrho}

for
x\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
\nu\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{S}^{1}
, where
\mathcal{H}_{n,\varepsilon}^{-}(u,A):=\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}(u,A)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{u}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{n}\cap A)
. Here, we recall the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\mathrm{PC}}_{\mathcal{H}_{n,\varepsilon}^{-}}
in (
5.2
)
and (
5.6
), respectively.

We notice that
[
31
, Theorem 2.4]
proves an integral representation result for the
\Gamma
-limit of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}
, and
[
31
, Remark 3.15]
shows that the surface density is exactly
h_{\varepsilon}^{-}
. Strictly speaking, the result has been explicitly detailed for
p=2
, but it holds with minor changes in the proof also for any
p>1
, cf.
[
31
, Remark 5.3]
.

As
\mathcal{H}_{n}^{-}\leq\mathcal{H}_{n,\varepsilon}^{-}
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,\varepsilon}^{-}
is increasing in
\varepsilon
, it is immediate that

h^{-}\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}h_{\varepsilon}^{-},

for
h^{-}
defined in (
5.5
). We observe that
H(x):=\limsup_{\varrho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n}\cap Q_{\varrho}^{\nu}(x))
is finite up to a set of negligible
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure. Indeed, if
H(x)=+\infty
on
B
with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(B)>0
, then the weak
∗
limit of
\mu_{n}:=\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}_{K_{n}}
, denoted by
\mu
, would satisfy
\mu(B)=+\infty
by
[
2
, Theorem 2.56]
, which contradicts the weak
∗
lower semicontinuity of the total variation
\mu(\Omega^{\prime})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mu_{n}(\Omega^{\prime})<+\infty
. Recalling (
5.5
) and (
5.20
) this yields

h_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x,\nu)\leq h^{-}(x,\nu)+\varepsilon H(x)\quad\text{for all $x\in\Omega^{\prime}$ and $\nu\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{S}^{1}$},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and then

h^{-}\geq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}h_{\varepsilon}^{-}.

Therefore, for any
A\in\mathcal{A}(\Omega^{\prime})
, the functionals
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,A)
pointwise (and monotonically) converge to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\cdot,A)
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n,\varepsilon}-\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}\in(0,M\varepsilon)
for
M:=\sup_{n}\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n})
, and then the representation result holds true also for the functional
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
given in (
5.18
). ∎

Proof of Proposition
5.8
.

(i) We first remark that
K\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
by Remark
5.2
(iii). By assumption we get
v|_{\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}}\in W^{1,p}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
v_{n}\to v\in L^{p}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, up to a subsequence. Let us apply the compactness result
[
31
, Theorem 3.8]
to
v_{n}
(which, similarly to the integral representation result employed in the proof of Lemma
5.9
, has been proven only for
p=2
but holds for every
p>1
, with minor changes in the proof). Recalling the precise form of the modifications, see
[
33
, Theorem 6.1]
and also
[
35
, Theorem 3.1]
, there are functions
y_{n}
with
y_{n}=v_{n}
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
r_{n}
with
|\{r_{n}\neq 0\}|\leq\frac{1}{n}
,
a_{n}=\sum_{j}a_{n}^{j}\chi_{P_{n}^{j}}\in{\rm PR}(\Omega^{\prime})
, and
u\in GSBD^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
such that

(5.21)

y_{n}=v_{n}+r_{n}+a_{n},

and

(5.22)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\big((J_{a_{n}}\cup J_{r_{n}})\setminus J_{v_{n}}\big)\leq\frac{1}{n},\qquad\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}(y_{n})\leq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}(v_{n})+\frac{1}{n},\qquad y_{n}\to u\text{ in measure on $\Omega^{\prime}$},

where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}
is defined as in (
5.17
). Since
a_{n}\in{\rm PR}(\Omega^{\prime})
and (
5.22
) holds, there exists
a=\sum_{j}a_{j}\chi_{P_{j}}\in{\rm PR}_{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
such that
\bar{\rm d}(a_{n},a)\to 0
by Proposition
5.7
. We notice that
a=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
by (
5.21
) and since
y_{n}=v_{n}
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
as well as
|\{r_{n}\neq 0\}|\to 0
. Next, we observe that

(5.23)

A^{\infty}_{v}=A^{\infty}_{a}.

Indeed, otherwise
|y_{n}|\to+\infty
a.e. on
A^{\infty}_{v}\triangle A^{\infty}_{a}
since
A^{\infty}_{v}=\{|v_{n}|\to+\infty\}
,
A^{\infty}_{a}=\{|a_{n}|\to+\infty\}
, and
|\{r_{n}\neq 0\}|\to 0
. This contradicts the third property in (
5.22
).
Since
a_{n}
,
a
are piecewise rigid functions, denoting by
B_{n}:=\bigcup\{P^{j}_{n}\colon P^{j}_{n}\cap A^{\infty}_{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}
, it holds that

(5.24)

|B_{n}\triangle A^{\infty}_{v}|\to 0,\qquad\partial^{*}B_{n}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{a_{n}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where for the second property we assumed without restriction that the affine mappings
(a_{n}^{j})_{j}
are pairwise distinct, cf.
[
34
, (3.1)]
. Then, for every
b\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

{y}^{b}_{n}:=y_{n}+b\chi_{B_{n}}-a_{n}\chi_{\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus B_{n}}

is such that, by (
5.21
)–(
5.24
) and the fact that
v_{n}
converges weakly in
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
to
v
,

(5.25)

{y}^{b}_{n}\to{u}^{b}:=v\chi_{\Omega^{\prime}\setminus A^{\infty}_{v}}+(u+b)\chi_{A^{\infty}_{v}}\quad\text{a.e.\ in }\Omega^{\prime},

and

(5.26)

\partial^{*}A^{\infty}_{v}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{{u}^{b}}\text{ for a.e.\ }b\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Let us fix some
b
satisfying (
5.26
). Recalling (
5.15
) and combining (
5.25
)–(
5.26
) we get

(5.27)

\displaystyle J_{v}\,\tilde{\subset}\,J_{{u}^{b}}.

We now apply the result
[
31
, Lemma 7.1]
to (the surface parts of)
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{n}
and
\mathcal{E}
as in Lemma
5.9
, and to the converging sequence
{y}^{b}_{n}\to u^{b}
, see (
5.25
): this gives that

\int_{J_{u^{b}}}h^{-}(x,\nu_{u^{b}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{y^{b}_{n}}\setminus K_{n}).

Using the definition of
y^{b}_{n}
, by the first property in (
5.22
), (
5.24
), and the assumption that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n})\to 0
, we get

\int_{J_{{u}^{b}}}h^{-}(x,\nu_{u^{b}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)=0.

By Definition
5.1
we find
J_{{u}^{b}}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
and by (
5.27
) we conclude the proof of (i).

(ii) Since
v_{n}\to v
in
L^{p}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, up to a subsequence we have
v_{n}\to v
a.e. in
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
and
|v_{n}|\to+\infty
a.e. in
A_{v}^{\infty}
, by definition of weak convergence in
GSBD^{p}_{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
. This shows
|(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega})\cap A_{v}^{\infty}|=0
. Moreover, the fact that
J_{{u}^{b}}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
and (
5.26
) give
\partial^{*}A^{\infty}_{v}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
.

Let
G_{K}
be the smallest set with (a)
|\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(\overline{\Omega}\cup G_{k})|=0
and (b)
\partial^{*}G_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K
. As
|(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega})\cap A_{v}^{\infty}|=0
, also
G_{K}\setminus A_{v}^{\infty}
is a set satisfying (a) and (b). Therefore, the minimality of
G_{K}
implies
|A^{\infty}_{v}\cap G_{K}|=0
.
∎

6.
Proof of the main result

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.2
. We start by establishing a uniform energy bound on
u_{n}(t)
, defined in (
4.18
), which will enable us to pass to the limit by the compactness and semicontinuity results that were established in the previous section. In order to conclude that (
4.14
) holds, we need a result on the stability of unilateral minimizers (Theorem
6.7
) whose proof is deferred to Section
7
. With this result at hand, we are finally able to conclude the proof of Theorem
4.2
.

6.1.
Energy bound

In this section, our goal is to show that the energy of the evolution is bounded uniformly in time. For this, we need to prove an energy estimate on the time-discrete level, which will be also crucial to establish the energy balance.

We recall the notation for the time discretization
\{0=t^{0}_{n}<t^{1}_{n}<\dots<t^{T/\delta_{n}}_{n}=T\}
of the interval
[0,T]
with step size
\delta_{n}
, and introduce the following shorthand notation. Given an arbitrary
v\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we write

(6.1)

\mathcal{E}^{0}_{n}(v)=E_{n}(v)\,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{k}_{n}(v)\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{n}(v,(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k})\quad\text{for}\,{k\geq 1,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

where
E_{n}
and
\mathcal{E}_{n}
are defined in (
4.3
) and (
4.7
), respectively, and
(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
denote the displacements that have been found at previous time steps
(t^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
.
We start by proving a bound on the elastic part of the energy.

Proposition 6.1

(Elastic energy)
.

Let
t\mapsto u_{n}(t)
be the discrete evolution defined in (
4.18
). There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
g
such that
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u^{0}_{n})\leq C
and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|e({u}_{n}(t))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq C\quad\text{ for all $t\in[0,T]$ and for all $n\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Proof.

For the time step
t_{n}^{0}=0
, we consider the background mesh
\mathbf{Z}_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\Omega^{\prime})
as introduced before (
4.4
) and the test function
g_{n}^{0}:=g(0)_{\mathbf{Z}_{n}}
, see (
4.2
).
We then have
g_{n}^{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{0}
, see (
4.9
). Since
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, we can deduce that
e(g_{n}^{0})_{T}
is uniformly bounded on each triangle
T
, hence we obtain
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(g_{n}^{0})\leq C
for some
C>0
independently of
n
. By (
4.10
) this implies that
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u_{n}^{0})\leq C
uniformly in
n
.

In a similar fashion, we can test with a function
g_{n}^{k}\in\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}
, namely
g_{n}^{k}=g(t_{n}^{k})_{\mathbf{T}_{n}}
, where
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is given by
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k-1})
. As before, the regularity of
g
implies that
e(g_{n}^{k})_{T}
is uniformly bounded, and thus
\varepsilon_{n}\,|e(g_{n}^{k})_{T}|^{2}<\kappa
for all
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}
for
n
large enough. In particular, recalling (
4.4
)–(
4.5
) this means
\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(g_{n}^{k})\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u_{n}^{k})
.
As
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{k}(u_{n}^{k})\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}^{k}(g_{n}^{k})
by (
4.11
), using (
4.7
) we can deduce for all
t\in[\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t_{n}^{k},t_{n}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|e({u}_{n}(t))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u_{n}^{k})}|e(u_{n}^{k})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(g_{n}^{k})}|e(g_{n}^{k})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\kappa\,\frac{|\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(g_{n}^{k})|}{\varepsilon_{n}}-\kappa\,\frac{|\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u_{n}^{k})|}{\varepsilon_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(g_{n}^{k})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|e(g_{n}^{k})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\,.

Eventually, we have
\|e(u_{n}(t))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega})}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C
by the definition of
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}
in (
4.9
) and the regularity of
g
.
In fact, if
T\cap\overline{\Omega}=\emptyset
, then
u_{n}(t)=g(t_{n}^{k})_{\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}(t))}
in
T
by the definition of
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{n}
. Instead, for
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}^{\rm bdy}_{n}(u_{n}(t))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}(t))\colon T\cap(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega})\neq\emptyset,\,T\cap\Omega\neq\emptyset\}
, it holds
\#\mathbf{T}^{\rm bdy}_{n}(u_{n}(t))\leq\frac{\tilde{C}}{\varepsilon_{n}}
with
\tilde{C}
depending on the Lipschitz constant of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Therefore, in view of (
4.4
),
\|e(u_{n}(t))\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega^{\rm bdy}_{n}(u_{n}(t)))}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{C}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, where

\Omega^{\rm bdy}_{n}(u_{n}(t))\coloneqq\mathrm{int}\Big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bdy}_{n}(u_{n}(t))}T\Big)\,.

This concludes the proof.
∎

As an immediate consequence, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
t\mapsto u_{n}(t)
be the discrete evolution defined in (
4.18
).
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
g
, but independent of
k
and
n
, such that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau\leq C\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{for all $k=0,\ldots,T/\delta_{n}$}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

By Hölder’s inequality we find

\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}e(u_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau\leq\,\|e(\partial_{t}g)\|_{L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\;\|e({u}_{n})\|_{L^{\infty}([0,t_{n}^{k});L^{2}(\Omega))}\,.

Using Proposition
6.1

and
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2}))
we deduce (
6.2
).
∎

We continue with the following discrete energy estimate that will be fundamental to establish a uniform bound on the energy and to prove the energy-balance law.

Lemma 6.3

(Discrete energy estimate)
.

Let
t\mapsto u_{n}(t)
be the discrete evolution defined in (
4.18
). Let
k=0,\ldots,T/\delta_{n}
. Then, there exists
(\beta_{n})_{n}
independent of
k
with
\beta_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
such that

(6.3)

{\mathcal{E}_{n}^{k}(u_{n}^{k})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u^{0}_{n})\leq 2\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}{g}(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau+\beta_{n}.}

Proof.

The argumentation follows a well-known strategy, see e.g.
[
26
, Section 3.2]
,
[
21
, Lemma 6.1]
, or
[
32
, Lemma 4.3]
for an application in a discrete setting. We notice that we have a quadratic bulk energy as in
[
26
]
, which simplifies the computations. We start by introducing a notation: for the boundary function
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and a time step
0\leq l\leq T/\delta_{n}
, we define
\tilde{g}_{n}^{l}(t):=g(t)_{\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{l})}
as the piecewise affine interpolation of
g(t)
with respect to
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{l})
.

Given the time
t_{n}^{l}
, we define the test function
\xi_{n}^{l}\coloneqq u_{n}^{l-1}+\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l})-\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1})
. Note that
\xi_{n}^{l}
is piecewise affine with respect to
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{l-1})
and by definition we have
\xi_{n}^{l}\in\mathcal{A}^{l}_{n}
. In view of (
4.11
), we obtain
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l})\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})
. Our goal is to prove that there exists a bounded sequence
(\vartheta_{n})_{n}
in
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega)
with
\|\vartheta_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\to 0
uniformly in
\tau
such that, for each
l
, we have

(6.4)

\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l-1})\leq 2\,\int^{t^{l}_{n}}_{t^{l-1}_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(e({u}_{n}(\tau)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\vartheta_{n}(\tau)):\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\,C\sigma^{l}_{n}{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,

where
\sigma^{l}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\int_{t^{l-1}_{n}}^{t^{l}_{n}}\|\partial_{t}g\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}\,{\rm d}s
. Once this is shown, we can deduce (
6.3
) as follows. Note first that
\mathcal{E}^{l-1}_{n}(u_{n}^{l-1})=\mathcal{E}^{l}_{n}(u_{n}^{l-1})
for each step
l
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l})\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})
, we can sum up over all time steps
1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq l\leq k
to obtain a telescopic sum on the left-hand side of (
6.4
) which leads to

(6.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{k}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u_{n}^{0})\leq 2\,\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}(e(u_{n}(\tau))+\vartheta_{n}(\tau)):\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},

where we used
\sum_{l}\sigma^{l}_{n}\leq C
. Since
\|\vartheta_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\to 0
uniformly in
\tau\in[0,T]
, we get by Hölder’s inequality

\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}|\vartheta_{n}(\tau):\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))|\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\|\vartheta_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to 0\,.

In view of (
6.5
), setting

\beta_{n}\coloneqq C{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{0}^{T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\Omega}|\vartheta_{n}(\tau):\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))|\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau,

we obtain (
6.3
).

It now remains to prove (
6.4
). By definition we have
\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(u_{n}^{l-1})=\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}\subset\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})
and hence (recall (
4.7
))

(6.6)

\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l-1})=\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})}|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}|e(u_{n}^{l-1})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

We can split the first term into

(6.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})}|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\,|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}x\,.

Note that
\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}
consists of all triangles
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}
, i.e., it holds that
\varepsilon_{n}|e(u_{n}^{j})_{T}|^{2}<\kappa
and
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u_{n}^{j}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}10^{6}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
for all
0\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}j\leq l-1
such that
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{j})
. Since
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\xi_{n}^{l})=\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{l-1})
, we hence have
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(\xi_{n}^{l}))=\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u_{n}^{l-1}))<10^{6}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
for all
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}
.
Therefore we conclude by (
4.4
) that
\varepsilon_{n}|e(\xi_{n}^{l})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa
for all
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}
. This leads to

\int_{\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\,|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq\kappa\frac{|\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}(\xi_{n}^{l})\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

Combining this with (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) we obtain

(6.8)

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l-1})\leq&\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}(|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}-|e(u_{n}^{l-1})|^{2})\,{\rm d}x\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\end{split}

Next, recalling the definition
\xi_{n}^{l}\coloneqq u_{n}^{l-1}+\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l})-\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1})
, we apply the mean value theorem to the function
h\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R};\,s\mapsto|e(u_{n}^{l-1})+\,s\,(e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l})-\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1})))|^{2}
to obtain some
\rho_{n}^{l-1}\in[0,1]
with

(6.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}-|e(u_{n}^{l-1})|^{2})\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle=2

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigl(e(u_{n}^{l-1})+\,\rho_{n}^{l-1}\,e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l})-\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1}))\bigr):\big(e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l})-\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1}))\big)\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.

We can now define the function
\vartheta_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[0,T]\to L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
by setting, for
s\in[t_{n}^{l-1},t_{n}^{l})
,

(6.10)

\vartheta_{n}(s)\coloneqq\rho_{n}^{l-1}\,\bigl(e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l}))-e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1}))\bigr)=\rho_{n}^{l-1}\,\int_{t_{n}^{l-1}}^{t_{n}^{l}}\partial_{t}e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(\tau))\,{\rm d}\tau\quad\text{ in $\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$}\,,

and
0
outside of it. Since
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, the function
\tau\mapsto\partial_{t}e(\tilde{g}^{l-1}_{n}(\tau))
belongs to
L^{1}(0,T;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))
and we can use the absolute continuity of the integral and
|t_{n}^{l}-t_{n}^{l-1}|=\delta_{n}\to 0
to conclude that
\|\vartheta_{n}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\to 0
uniformly in
s
.
Moreover, we write

(6.11)

\displaystyle e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l}))-e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(t_{n}^{l-1}))=\int_{t_{n}^{l-1}}^{t_{n}^{l}}\partial_{t}e(g(\tau))\,{\rm d}\tau+\zeta_{n}^{l-1}\,,

with

\zeta_{n}^{l-1}\coloneqq\int_{t_{n}^{l-1}}^{t_{n}^{l}}\partial_{t}e(\tilde{g}_{n}^{l-1}(\tau)-g(\tau))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau\,
.
By the regularity of
g
and the definition of
\tilde{g}_{n}^{l}(t)
we obtain

(6.12)

\|\zeta_{n}^{l-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C\sigma^{l}_{n}\,\omega(\varepsilon_{n})\leq C\sigma^{l}_{n}\,\varepsilon_{n},

where
\sigma^{l}_{n}
is given in (
6.4
), and the last step follows from (
4.1
). Noting that
\vartheta_{n}
is piecewise constant in time, we deduce from (
6.9
)–(
6.11
) and by Fubini’s theorem

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(|e(\xi_{n}^{l})|^{2}-|e(u_{n}^{l-1})|^{2})\,{\rm d}x=2

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}^{l-1}}^{t_{n}^{l}}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e(u_{n}^{l-1})+\vartheta_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e(u_{n}^{l-1})+\vartheta_{n}(\tau)):\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\zeta_{n}^{l-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}x\,.

By Proposition
6.1
, the definition in (
4.18
), and the fact that
\|\vartheta_{n}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\to 0
, we can estimate the last term by (
6.12
) and obtain

(6.14)

\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,l-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e(u_{n}^{l-1})+\vartheta_{n}(\tau)):\zeta_{n}^{l-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}x\leq C\sigma^{l}_{n}\,\varepsilon_{n}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

By (
4.18
), (
6.8
), (
6.13
), and (
6.14
) we obtain

\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(\xi_{n}^{l})-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{l}(u_{n}^{l-1})\leq 2\int_{t_{n}^{l-1}}^{t_{n}^{l}}\int_{\Omega}(e({u}_{n}(\tau))+\vartheta_{n}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0})):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\tau+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\sigma^{l}_{n}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.

This yields (
6.4
) and concludes the proof.
∎

As a direct consequence, we obtain the following bound on the energy.

Corollary 6.4

(Energy bound)
.

Let
t\mapsto u_{n}(t)
be the discrete evolution defined in (
4.18
). Then, there exists a constant
C>0
only depending on
g
such that

\mathcal{E}^{k}_{n}(u^{k}_{n})\leq C\quad\text{for all $k=0,\ldots,T/\delta_{n}$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Proof.

The proof follows by combining
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u^{0}_{n})\leq C
(see Proposition
6.1
), (
6.2
), and Lemma
6.3
.
∎

6.2.
Compactness and lower semicontinuity

Based on the energy bound in Corollary
6.4
, we can pass to the limit in the crack sets and displacements by compactness arguments. We start with the crack sets.

Proposition 6.5

(Convergence of crack sets)
.

Let
t\mapsto(u_{n}(t),K_{n}(t))
be the evolution defined in (
4.18
) and (
4.19
). There exists an increasing set function
t\mapsto K(t)
in
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
and a subsequence (not relabeled) such that, for every
t\in[0,T]
, the set
K_{n}(t)

\sigma
-converges to
K(t)
.

Proof.

By (
4.17
), (
4.19
), and the energy bound in Corollary
6.4
we have that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n}(t))\leq C
for all
t\in[0,T]
. We therefore can apply Proposition
5.3
for each
t\in[0,T]
to obtain the limiting set function
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Omega^{\prime}
such that
K_{n}(t)

\sigma
-converges to
K(t)
for a
t
-dependent subsequence. Since
K_{n}(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
, the definition of
\sigma
-convergence directly implies
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Omega^{\prime}\cap\overline{\Omega}
, see Remark
5.2
(iii). Our goal is to apply Helly’s theorem in the version of Theorem
5.4
to show that the subsequence can be chosen independently of
t
and that
t\mapsto K(t)
is increasing. This is however impeded by the fact that
K_{n}(t)
is not an increasing set function. As a remedy, we define

\tilde{K}_{n}(t)\coloneqq\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm mod}}\partial T\,\quad\text{for}\;t\in[t_{n}^{k},t_{n}^{k+1})\,,

where
\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm mod}:=\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\colon T\subset\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\}
. As
\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\subset\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
, see (
4.6
), we get
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm mod}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
by Corollary
3.1
, and thus the set function
t\mapsto\tilde{K}_{n}(t)
in fact fulfills
\tilde{K}_{n}(s)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{K}_{n}(t)
for
s\leq t
. Moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\tilde{K}_{n}(t))\leq C
for all
t\in[0,T]
, again by the energy bound in Corollary
6.4
. (Use
|T|\geq c\varepsilon\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial T)
for all triangles
T
for
c
depending on
\theta_{0}
to get
\#\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\leq C/\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, as well as (
4.1
).) Hence, we can apply Theorem
5.4
to obtain an increasing set function
t\mapsto\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
\tilde{K}_{n}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\sigma
-converges to
\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for every
t\in[0,T]
, up to extracting a subsequence (not relabeled). We now want to show that
\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{=}\,K(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
.
Note that each triangle
T\in\{T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\colon T\subset\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\}
fulfills
\partial T\cap K_{n}\,\tilde{=}\,\emptyset
, by Remark
3.5
. Therefore, we have
K_{n}(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\tilde{K}_{n}(t)
because

K_{n}(t)=\partial\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\setminus\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\\
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\end{subarray}}\partial T\subset\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\\
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\end{subarray}}\partial T=\tilde{K}_{n}(t)\,.

By Remark
5.2
(ii), this implies
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. To show the reverse inclusion, we fix a time
t\in[0,T]
. We denote by
\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{K}(t)}^{-}
the
\Gamma
-limit of
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\tilde{K}_{n}(t)}
given by
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\tilde{K}_{n}(t)}(u,A)=\mathcal{H}^{1}((J_{u}\setminus\tilde{K}_{n}(t))\cap A)
, and let
h^{-}_{\tilde{K}(t)}
be the corresponding density function from (
5.3
). In the analogous way, we denote
\mathcal{H}_{{K}(t)}^{-}
and
h^{-}_{{K}(t)}
.

We suppose by contradiction that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\tilde{K}(t)\setminus K(t))>0
. As
K(t)
is the maximal set on which
h^{-}_{K(t)}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e. equal to
0
(cf. Definition
5.1
), we find
\mu>0
such that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{K}(t)}h^{-}_{K(t)}(x,\nu_{\tilde{K}(t)}(x))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)>3\mu.

We use a covering argument to find an open set
U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and a function
v\in{\rm PC}(U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
such that

(6.16)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus U)\leq\mu\quad\quad\mathcal{H}^{1}\big((\tilde{K}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\triangle\,J_{v})\cap U\big)\leq\mu\,.

Let
(v_{n})_{n}
be a recovery sequence for
v
with respect to the functional
\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{K}(t)}^{-}(\cdot,U)
, i.e., particularly

{\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}((J_{v_{n}}\setminus\tilde{K}_{n}(t))\cap U)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{v}\cap U}h^{-}_{\tilde{K}(t)}(x,\nu_{v}(x))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}

by (
6.16
) and the fact that
h^{-}_{\tilde{K}(t)}\leq 1
, see Remark
5.2
(i). We define a modified sequence by

(6.17)

\tilde{v}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\coloneqq\begin{cases}v_{n}(x)&x\in\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod},\\
0&x\in\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\,.\end{cases}

Then, recalling (
4.19
) we have

J_{\tilde{v}_{n}}\setminus K_{n}(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,J_{v_{n}}\setminus\tilde{K}_{n}(t)
and thus
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}((J_{\tilde{v}_{n}}\setminus K_{n}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\mu
. In view of (
4.16
)–(
4.17
), we obtain
|\{\tilde{v}_{n}\neq v_{n}\}|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}|\leq C_{\eta_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
. As
{v}_{n}\to v
in
L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, we thus get
\tilde{v}_{n}\to v
in
L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Using the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality for the
\sigma
-convergence of
K_{n}(t)
to
K(t)
we get

(6.18)

\mathcal{H}_{K(t)}^{-}(v,U)=\int_{J_{v}\cap U}h^{-}_{K(t)}(x,\nu_{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}((J_{\tilde{v}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\setminus K_{n}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\mu.

Now, (
6.16
) and the fact that
h^{-}_{K(t)}\leq 1
show

\int_{\tilde{K}(t)}h^{-}_{K(t)}(x,\nu_{\tilde{K}(t)}(x))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}(x)\leq\mathcal{H}_{K(t)}^{-}(v,U)+2\mu\leq 3\mu,

which contradicts (
6.15
) and concludes the proof. ∎

In the sequel, it will be convenient to express some of the quantities considered so far in terms of the time
t
in place of the iteration step. As before, let
\{0=t^{0}_{n}<t^{1}_{n}<\dots<t^{T/\delta_{n}}_{n}=T\}
be the time discretization of the interval
[0,T]
, and let
(u^{j}_{n})_{j<k}
be a corresponding displacement history. We define the set of functions
\mathcal{A}_{n}(t)\coloneqq\mathcal{A}_{n}^{k}
for
t\in[t^{k}_{n},t_{n}^{k+1})
, see (
4.9
). Recalling (
4.7
) and (
6.1
), we define

(6.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}(v_{n};t):=\mathcal{E}_{n}^{k}(v_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n}(v_{n};(u_{n}^{j})_{j<k})\quad\text{for}\;t\in[t^{k}_{n},t_{n}^{k+1})

for each
v_{n}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}(t)
. We use similar notation for the parts of the energy introduced in (
4.7
). Recall also the Griffith energy defined in (
4.12
).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
t\mapsto(u_{n}(t),K_{n}(t))
be the evolution defined in (
4.18
) and (
4.19
). Let
K(t)
be the
\sigma
-limit of
K_{n}(t)
given by Proposition
6.5
, and let
G(t)
be the set corresponding to
K(t)
