defined before (
4.20
). Then, there exists a function
u(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in AD(g(t),K(t))
with
u(t)=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
, and a subsequence
(n_{l})_{l}
depending on
t
, such that
u_{n_{l}}(t)\to u(t)
in measure on
G(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
e(u_{n_{l}}(t))\rightharpoonup e(u(t))
weakly in
L^{2}(G(t);\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
.
Moreover,

(6.20)

{\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\geq\kappa\sin\theta_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(K(t))\,.}

Proof.

We fix
t\in[0,T]
and for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
we choose
k_{n}
such that
t\in[t^{k_{n}}_{n},t^{k_{n}+1}_{n})
. Recall the definition of
\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm crack}
and
\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm mod}
in (
4.6
) and (
4.17
), respectively. By
u^{\rm mod}_{n}
we denote the function given by Theorem
2.1
applied on
u_{n}(t)
, which by (
2.13
) and the energy bounds in Proposition
6.1
, and Corollary
6.4
satisfies

(6.21)

\displaystyle\|e(u^{\rm mod}_{n})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n},\eta_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm mod})}\leq C_{\eta_{n}}.

Choose
\Omega_{*}\subset\subset\Omega^{\prime}
and suppose that
n
is large enough such that
\Omega_{*}\subset\Omega^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n},\eta_{n}}
. We define
v_{n}\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega_{*}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
by
v_{n}\coloneqq(1-\chi_{\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm mod}})u^{\rm mod}_{n}
. Then, by (
4.16
), (
4.17
), and Corollary
6.4
we get

(6.22)

\displaystyle|D_{n}|\leq C_{\eta_{n}}\varepsilon_{n}+C\varepsilon_{n}\leq C_{\eta_{n}}\varepsilon_{n}\to 0,\quad\text{ where }D_{n}:=\big(\{u_{n}(t)\neq v_{n}\}\cap\Omega_{*}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm crack}.

For
p:=3/2
we have by Hölder’s inequality, (
6.21
)–(
6.22
), and the energy bound in Proposition
6.1
that

\displaystyle\|e({v}_{n})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{*}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}

\displaystyle=\|e(v_{n})\|_{L^{p}(D_{n})}+\|e(u_{n}^{k_{n}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm crack})}

\displaystyle\leq C|D_{n}|^{1/6}\|e(u^{\rm mod}_{n})\|_{L^{2}(D_{n}\setminus\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm mod})}+C\|e(u_{n}^{k_{n}})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm crack})}\leq C_{\eta_{n}}^{7/6}\varepsilon_{n}^{1/6}+C\leq C,

where in the last step we used (
4.16
). By (
4.17
) and the energy bound we get
\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{{v}_{n}})\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n}(t))\leq C
. Thus, by Proposition
5.7
we find
u(t)\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Omega_{*})
such that (up to a subsequence, not relabeled)

(6.23)

v_{n}\to u(t)\text{ in measure on }\Omega_{*}\setminus A_{u(t)}^{\infty}.

By Proposition
5.8
(i) and the
\sigma
-convergence of
K_{n}(t)
to
K(t)
, we get
J_{u(t)}\cap\Omega_{*}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(t)
. Then, by a diagonal argument (letting
\Omega_{*}\nearrow\Omega^{\prime}
), we find
u(t)\in GSBD^{p}_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Omega^{\prime})
such that
v_{n}\to u(t)
in measure on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus A_{u(t)}^{\infty}
with
J_{u(t)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(t)
.

Note that
g(t_{n}^{k_{n}})_{\mathbf{T}(u_{n}^{k_{n}})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to g(t)
in
W^{1,2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
by the regularity of
g
(recall (
4.2
)). As
u_{n}(t)\in\mathcal{A}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
4.9
)), it is elementary to check that
u(t)=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
.
In particular, we find
A_{u(t)}^{\infty}\subset\Omega
. Then, we can apply Proposition
5.8
(ii) which along with the definition of
G(t)
before (
4.20
) implies
|A_{u(t)}^{\infty}\cap G(t)|=\emptyset
. Thus, in view of (
6.23
), we conclude
v_{n}\to u(t)
in measure on
G(t)
.
Next, by (
6.22
) we get
u_{n}(t)\to u(t)
in measure on
G(t)
. Moreover, by Proposition
6.1
,
|\Omega_{n,k_{n}}^{\rm crack}|\to 0
, and by weak compactness we get
e(u_{n}(t))\rightharpoonup e(u(t))
weakly in
L^{2}(G(t);\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
.
Up to replacing
u(t)
by
0
in
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
(not relabeled), we thus obtain
u(t)\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
, and
J_{u(t)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(t)
as well as
u(t)=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
still hold. Summarizing, we have shown that
u(t)\in AD(g(t),K(t))
, see (
4.13
).

It remains to show (
6.20
).
We recall from (
4.17
) and (
4.19
) that

\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{n}(t))\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}\sin\theta_{0}}|\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}|+C\eta_{n}\leq\frac{2}{\kappa\sin\theta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)+C\eta_{n}.

For
n\to\infty
we have
\eta_{n}\to 0
and
|\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k}|\to 0
(see (
4.16
)–(
4.17
)). Then, in view of
K_{n}(t)=\partial\Omega^{\rm mod}_{n,k}
, by means of Lemma
5.6
we derive

2\mathcal{H}^{1}(K(t))\leq\frac{2}{\kappa\sin\theta_{0}}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{\rm crack}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\,.

This concludes the proof of (
6.20
).
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.2

In this section we give the proof of the main result. We need a final preparation. Recall the Griffith energy
\mathcal{E}
defined in (
4.12
).

Theorem 6.7

(Stability)
.

Let
t\mapsto(u_{n}(t),K_{n}(t))
be the evolution defined by (
4.18
)–(
4.19
) and let
K(t)
be the
\sigma
-limit of
K_{n}(t)
.
For any
\psi\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
with
\psi=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}

there exists a sequence
(\psi_{n})_{n}
of piecewise affine displacements with
\psi_{n}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{A}_{n}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
converging to
\psi
in measure on
\Omega^{\prime}
such that

(6.24)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\,\Big(\ \mathcal{E}^{{\rm crack}}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\psi_{n};t)-\mathcal{E}^{{\rm crack}}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\Big)\leq\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\setminus K(t)),

and

(6.25)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\,\mathcal{E}^{{\rm elast}}_{n}(\psi_{n};t)\leq\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x.

Corollary 6.8

(Recovery sequence)
.

For each
\psi\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
with
\psi=g(0)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
there exists a sequence
(\psi_{n})_{n}
with
\psi_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{0}_{n}
such that
\psi_{n}
converges to
\psi
in measure on
\Omega^{\prime}
and

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{n}(\psi_{n})\leq\mathcal{E}(\psi,J_{\psi}).

This result is essential to pass from the minimality condition (
4.11
) in the finite element model to the global stability in (
4.14
). For this reason, estimates of this kind are often referred to as
stability of unilateral minimizers
, see e.g.
[
40
]
. The proof of Theorem
6.7
will be deferred to the next section; it could be directly adapted (indeed, simplified: it is enough to drop the dependence on
t
and formally consider
K_{n}=\emptyset
) to prove Corollary
6.8
, which in turn confirms the upper bound in the
\Gamma
-convergence result
[
3
, Theorem 3.1]
. The strategy follows the one of the
jump transfer lemma
introduced by
Francfort and Larsen
in
[
26
]
, see the works
[
21
,
23
,
32
,
33
,
37
]
for several variants. Our situation is slightly different and more delicate because we have to construct an admissible mesh associated to the recovery sequence. For this, we will exploit the ideas by
Dal Maso and Chambolle

[
14
]
for the explicit construction of a minimizing adaptive mesh.

We are now in the position to prove the main result of this paper.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

We split the proof into five parts. First, we prove the existence of a limiting evolution and validate the irreversibility of the crack sets. Subsequently, in a second step, we use Theorem
6.7
to prove the stability property (
4.14
). Afterwards, we show the convergence of displacement fields without the necessity of passing to
t
-dependent subsequences, see Step 3. Next, we want to confirm that the mapping
t\to(u(t),K(t))

actually fulfills the energy balance (
4.15
) which is content of Step 4. In the last step, we then prove the convergence of energies and thus the strong convergence of the linear strains.

Step 1: Limiting evolution.
Let
K(t)
be the
\sigma
-limit of
K_{n}(t)
for
t\in[0,T]
given by Proposition
6.5
. Note that
t\mapsto K(t)
is an increasing set function. For each fixed time
t\in[0,T]
, by virtue of Proposition
6.6
, there exists
u(t)\in AD(g(t),K(t))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with
u(t)=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
and a subsequence
n_{l}
depending on
t

such that
u_{n_{l}}(t)
converges to
u(t)
in measure on
G(t)
and
e(u_{n_{l}}(t))\rightharpoonup e(u(t))
weakly in
L^{2}(G(t);\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
.

Step 2: Stability (
4.14
).

Fix
t\in[0,T]
. Let
H
with
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,H
and
\psi\in AD(g(t),H)
. We employ Theorem
6.7
to obtain a sequence of piecewise affine displacements
\psi_{n}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
approximating
\psi
and satisfying (
6.24
)–(
6.25
).
By the minimality property of the solution
u_{n}(t)
, see (
4.10
)–(
4.11
), (
4.18
), and the shorthand notation in (
6.19
) we have

\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}(\psi_{n};t)\,.

We now split the energy on both sides like in (
4.7
): subtracting the crack energy on the left-hand side gives us

(6.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}^{{\rm{elast}}}(u_{n}(t);t)\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}^{{\rm{elast}}}(\psi_{n};t)+\mathcal{E}_{n}^{{\rm{crack}}}(\psi_{n};t)-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{{\rm{crack}}}(u_{n}(t);t).

Passing to the limit in (
6.26
), by employing (
6.24
)
and (
6.25
), we find

(6.27)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{n}^{{\rm{elast}}}(u_{n}(t);t)\leq\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\setminus K(t)).

By Proposition
6.1
, Proposition
6.6
, and a compactness argument, we get
e(u_{n_{l}}(t))\rightharpoonup W(t)
in
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
for some
W(t)\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
with
W(t)=e(u(t))
on
G(t)
. As
u(t)=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
, we
derive

(6.28)

\int_{\Omega}|e(u(t))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega\setminus G(t)}|W(t)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\setminus K(t)).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Since
\psi\in AD(\psi,H)
, we have
J_{\psi}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}H
, and thus

(6.29)

\int_{\Omega}|e(u(t))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(H\setminus K(t)).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Because
K(t)\,\tilde{\subset}\,H
we conclude that (
4.14
) holds. By particularly choosing
\psi=u(t)
in (
6.28
) and recalling
J_{u(t)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(t)
, we get
W(t)\equiv 0
on
\Omega\setminus G(t)
, and thus
e(u_{n_{l}}(t))\rightharpoonup e(u(t))
weakly in
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
.

Step 3: Uniqueness of limiting displacements.
We argue that the obtained limit
u(t)
is uniquely determined on
G(t)
and satisfies
e(u(t))=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. This along with Uyrsohn’s principle shows that the subsequence
(n_{l})_{l}
in Step 1 can be chosen independently of
t
. This shows (
4.22
) except for strong convergence, which we defer to Step 5.

Let us suppose that
\hat{u}(t)\in AD(g(t),K(t))
denotes another limit of the sequence
u_{n}(t)
found in Step 1. As in Step 2, one can show that (
6.29
) holds for
\hat{u}(t)
in place of
u(t)
. This shows that both
u(t)
and
\hat{u}(t)
are minimizers of the strictly convex minimization problem
v\mapsto\int_{\Omega}|e(v)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x
for
v\in AD(g(t),K(t))
. Consequently,
e(u(t))=e(\hat{u}(t))
on
\Omega^{\prime}
. Moreover,
e(u(t))=e(\hat{u}(t))=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus G(t)
, see (
4.20
). By a piecewise rigidity argument (see
[
15
]
), taking the definition of
G(t)
and the fact
u(t)=\hat{u}(t)=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}\subset G(t)
into account and using the fact that adding infinitesimal rigid motions is only admissible on the connected components of
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus K(t)
inside
B(t)
, we then see that
u(t)=\hat{u}(t)
on
G(t)
. We refer to
[
35
, Proof of Theorem 2.2]
for details.

Moreover, arguing as in Step 2 and above in Step 3 (cf. also
[
26
, Lemma 3.8]
and
[
33
, Theorem 7.5]
), we can ensure that, for each
t\in[0,T]
outside of an at most countable subset and every
t_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nearrow\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t
, we have that
u(t_{n})\to u(t)
in measure on
G(t)
and
e(u(t_{n}))\rightharpoonup e(u(t))
in
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2})
.

Step 4: Energy balance:
From Step 1 and Step 3 we recall that
e({u}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\tau))\rightharpoonup e(u(\tau))
in
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
for all
\tau\in[0,T]
. As
e(\partial_{t}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau,\cdot\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
is uniformly bounded in time, using Proposition
6.1
we can apply the reverse Fatou’s lemma to deduce that

(6.30)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}(\tau)):(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e(u(\tau)):(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau\,.

By Lemma
6.3
we find
(\beta_{n})_{n}
with
\beta_{n}\to 0
such that for any
t\in[t_{n}^{k},t_{n}^{k+1})
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u^{0}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t_{n}^{k}}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}{g}(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau+\beta_{n}.

By passing to another sequence
(\beta_{n})_{n}
, still satisfying
\beta_{n}\to 0
, for every
t\in[0,T]
we have

(6.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau+C\beta_{n}+\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u^{0}_{n})\,,

where we have used
e({u}_{n})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}_{\rm sym}^{2\times 2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))
(see Proposition
6.1
) and
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2}))
to estimate the integral from
t_{n}^{k}
to
t
in terms of
\beta_{n}
.
Recalling (
4.10
), (
6.1
), and Corollary
6.8
we have

\mathcal{E}(u(0),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K(0)\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{n}(u_{n}^{0})=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\min_{\mathcal{A}^{0}_{n}}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{n}\leq\mathcal{E}(\psi,J_{\psi})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for all
\psi\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with
\psi=g(0)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
, where the first inequality follows from Proposition
6.6
and weak lower semicontinuity of norms. As
J_{u(0)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(0)
, this shows that condition (a) in Definition
4.1
holds. By choosing
\psi=u(0)
and using again
J_{u(0)}\,\tilde{\subset}\,K(0)
, we get
J_{u(0)}=K(0)
and
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{n}(u_{n}^{0})\leq\mathcal{E}(u(0),K(0))
. Combining this with Proposition
6.6
and (
6.30
)–(
6.31
) we get

(6.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(u(t),K(t))\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{n}^{0}(u_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\limsup_{n\to\infty}2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e({u}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}(u(0),K(0))+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e(u(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau.

Thus, it remains to prove the reverse inequality

(6.33)

\mathcal{E}(u(t),K(t))\geq\,\mathcal{E}(u(0),K(0))+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e(u(\tau)):e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\tau.

Fixed
t\in[0,T]
, let
(s_{k}^{i})_{i}
be a partition of
[0,t]
with

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\max_{1\leq i\leq k}(s_{k}^{i}-s_{k}^{i-1})=0.

For any
i
and
k
, we take
H=K(s_{k}^{i})
and
v=(u(s_{k}^{i})-g(s_{k}^{i})+g(s_{k}^{i-1}))\in AD(g(s_{k}^{i-1}),K(s_{k}^{i}))
as an admissible competitor at time
s_{k}^{i-1}
. This yields

{\mathcal{E}(u(s_{k}^{i-1}),K(s_{k}^{i-1})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\mathcal{E}\big(u(s_{k}^{i})+\big(g(s_{k}^{i-1})-g(s_{k}^{i})\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0},K(s_{k}^{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big),}

that is

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}(u(s_{k}^{i}),K(s_{k}^{i}))\geq\mathcal{E}(u(s_{k}^{i-1}),K(s_{k}^{i-1}))+2\int_{\Omega}e(u(s_{k}^{i}))\colon e(g(s_{k}^{i})-g(s_{k}^{i-1}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|e(g(s_{k}^{i})-g(s_{k}^{i-1}))|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Summing over
i
, and observing that
g(s_{k}^{i})-g(s_{k}^{i-1})=\int_{s_{k}^{i-1}}^{s_{k}^{i}}\partial_{t}g(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau
are functions on
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2})
(where the integral is in the Bochner sense), we get

\mathcal{E}(u(t),K(t))\geq\mathcal{E}(u(0),K(0))+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}e(\overline{u}(\tau))\colon e(\partial_{t}g(\tau))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tau+o_{k\to+\infty}(1),

with
\overline{u}(\tau):=u(s_{k}^{i})
for
\tau\in(s_{k}^{i-1},s_{k}^{i}]
. Now, we use
g\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2}))

together with the uniform bound from Proposition
6.1
and the weak convergence in
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
, as
k\to\infty
, of
e(\overline{u}(\tau))
to
e(u(\tau))
for all
\tau\in[0,t]
, except for at most countable many (see end of Step 3). This shows (
6.33
) and
concludes the proof of the energy balance (
4.15
).

Step 5: Energy convergence and strong convergence of displacements
.
Gathering (
6.32
) with (
6.33
), and recalling (
6.19
) we deduce (
4.23
). Moreover, since the two parts of the energy are separately lower semicontinuous, we obtain the statement in Remark
4.3
(i). In particular,
\|e({u}_{n}(t))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\to\|e(u(t))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
, which shows
e(u_{n}(t))\to e(u(t))
strongly in
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{\rm sym})
and concludes the proof of (
4.22
). ∎

7.
Unilateral stability: Proof of Theorem
6.7

This section is entirely devoted to the proof of the stability result in Theorem
6.7
.

7.1.
Preparations

We start with some preparations for the proof.

Density argument

We first observe that it suffices to prove the statement for functions
\psi
with more regularity, employing a suitable density argument.
Let
\mathcal{W}(\Omega^{\prime})\subset GSBD^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
be the collection of functions
v
such that
J_{v}
is closed and included in a finite union of closed and connected pieces of
C^{1}
-curves and
v
lies in
W^{2,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus J_{v};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. By the density result
[
35
, Theorem 3.2]
we can choose a sequence
(v_{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{W}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with
v_{n}=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
such that

(7.1)

\begin{cases}\text{$v_{n}\to v$ in measure on $\Omega^{\prime}$}\,,\\
\|e(v_{n})-e(v)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})}\to 0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,\\
\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v_{n}}\triangle J_{v})\to 0.\end{cases}

Bearing in mind this density result, by a diagonal argument it suffices to construct a sequence as in Theorem
6.7
for a function
\psi\in\mathcal{W}(\Omega^{\prime})
with
\psi=g(t)
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
. Furthermore, for simplicity we only treat the case that
J_{\psi}\cap\partial_{D}\Omega=\emptyset
(no jump along the boundary), for the general case follows by minor adaptations of the construction at the boundary (see
[
26
]
) which would merely overburden notation in the sequel.

Choose
k(t)\in\mathbb{N}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
t\in[t^{k(t)}_{n},t^{k(t)+1}_{n})
and define
\mathcal{A}^{k(t)}_{n}(g(t))
as in (
4.9
) with
g(t)
in place of
g(t^{k}_{n})
. We fix
\theta>0
and observe that it suffices to construct a sequence
(\psi_{n})_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{k(t)}_{n}(g(t))

of displacements such that

(7.2)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}|\{|\psi_{n}-\psi|\geq\delta\}|\leq C\theta\quad\text{for all}\;\delta>0\,,

(7.3)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\,\mathcal{E}^{{\rm crack}}_{n}(\psi_{n};t)-\mathcal{E}^{{\rm crack}}_{n}(u_{n}(t);t)\leq\kappa\sin(\theta_{0})\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\setminus K(t))+C\theta\,,

(7.4)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\,\mathcal{E}^{{\rm elast}}_{n}(\psi_{n};t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+C\theta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where
C>0
is a universal constant. Strictly speaking, we need to construct a sequence
\psi_{n}
that attains the boundary values
g(t^{k(t)}_{n})
, i.e., lies in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{A}_{n}(t)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\mathcal{A}^{k(t)}_{n}=\mathcal{A}^{k(t)}_{n}(g(t^{k(t)}_{n}))
. Therefore, we eventually need to replace the sequence
(\psi_{n})_{n}
by
\psi_{n}-g(t)_{\mathbf{T}(\psi_{n})}+g(t^{k(t)}_{n})_{\mathbf{T}(\psi_{n})}
. Due to the regularity of
g
, this still leads to (
7.2
)–(
7.4
). Then, the statement follows again by a diagonal argument, sending
\theta\to 0
.

Besicovitch covering

We follow the procedure from
[
32
]
, which in turn stems from

[
26
]
, i.e., we introduce a fine cover of

J_{\psi}
with closed squares satisfying certain additional properties. By
\nu_{\psi}
we denote a measure-theoretic unit normal at
J_{\psi}
. We split our considerations into (1)
K(t)\cap J_{\psi}
and (2)
J_{\psi}\setminus K(t)
.

(1) By a covering argument, for given
\theta>0
there exists an open set
U\subset\Omega^{\prime}
and a function
v\in{\rm PC}(U)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that

(7.5)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(K(t)\setminus U)\leq\theta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad\mathcal{H}^{1}((K(t)\,\triangle\,J_{v})\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\theta\,.

By the
\sigma
-convergence of
K_{n}(t)
to
K(t)
, there exists a sequence
(v_{n})_{n}\in PC(U)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with
v_{n}\to v
in
L^{1}(U)
and

(7.6)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n}(t))\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v}\setminus K(t))\leq\theta\,,

see (
5.8
) for a similar argument. We can choose a suitable subset
G_{j}\subset J_{v}
as done preceding
[
26
, (2.2)]
with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus G_{j})\leq\theta
such that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(K(t)\setminus G_{j})\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}((K(t)\setminus G_{j})\cap U)+\theta\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus G_{j})+2\theta\leq 3\theta\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

For each
x\in G_{j}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we consider closed squares
Q_{r}(x)
with sidelength
2r
and two sides orthogonal to
\nu_{\psi}(x)
which are contained in
U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and satisfy
[
26
, (2.3), (2.5)]
.

(2) For closed squares
Q_{r}(x)\subset\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with a center
x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K(t)
, still oriented in direction
\nu_{\psi}(x)
, we can assume that for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K(t)
and for
r
sufficiently small it holds

(7.8)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(K(t)\cap Q_{r}(x)\big)\leq\theta r\,.

Here, we use the fact that
K(t)
has
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-density
0
in a subset of
J_{\psi}\setminus K(t)
of full
\mathcal{H}^{1}
measure.

As
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is contained in a finite
union of closed
C^{1}
-curves, for a.e.
x\in J_{\psi}
, possibly passing to smaller
r
, the above squares in cases (1) and (2) can be chosen such that they also satisfy

(7.9)

\displaystyle 2r

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{r}(x)\big)\leq 4r,

(7.10)

\displaystyle J_{\psi}\cap Q_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x)

\displaystyle\subset\{y\colon|(y-x)\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x)|\leq\theta r\}.

With this, we obtain a fine cover of
\Gamma:=(G_{j}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cup(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K(t))
to which we can apply the Besicovitch covering theorem with respect to the Radon measure
\mathcal{L}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{1}|_{\Gamma}
. For
\theta>0
fixed as above, we hereby find a finite and disjoint subcollection
\mathcal{B}:=(Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i}))_{i}
, or shortly denoted by
(Q_{i})_{i}
, such that
(Q_{i})_{i}
satisfy the properties (
7.8
)–(
7.10
) ((
7.8
) only for
x_{i}\notin K(t)
) as well as

(7.11)

\mathcal{L}^{2}\big(\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}Q_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\leq\theta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad\quad\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\Gamma\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}Q_{i}\big)\leq\theta\,.

Here and in the following, we use
\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}}Q_{i}
as a shorthand for
\bigcup_{Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\in\mathcal{B}}Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})
. Using (
7.7
), (
7.11
), and the definition of
\Gamma
we get

(7.12)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}Q_{i}\big)\leq 4\theta.

Without further notice, we will frequently use that the squares are pairwise disjoint.

By
\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}\subset\mathcal{B}
we denote the collection of closed squares
Q_{i}=Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})
with
x_{i}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K(t)
, and similarly we let
\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\subset\mathcal{B}
be the collection of all squares
Q_{i}=Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})
with
x_{i}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Clearly, we have
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
. We also define the sets

B_{\rm good}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}}Q_{i},\quad\quad\quad B_{\rm bad}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}Q_{i}.

Note that by construction we have
B_{\rm bad}\subset U
. In order to construct a sequence
\psi_{n}\colon\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
of piecewise affine functions satisfying (
7.2
)–(
7.4
), we need to specify the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
that is associated to
\psi_{n}
. For this, we split
\Omega^{\prime}
into three different subsets
B_{\rm good}
,
B_{\rm bad}
and
B_{\rm rest}=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(B_{\rm good}\cup B_{\rm bad})
. Inside of
B_{\rm good}
and
B_{\rm bad}
we will need two kinds of a
transfer of jump sets
which we discuss next.

Jump transfer

We again consider the good and bad squares separately.

Bad squares

The sequence
(v_{n})_{n}\subset PC(U)
defined above, satisfying
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{v_{n}}\setminus K_{n}(t))\leq\theta
and
v_{n}\to v
in
L^{1}(U;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, can be used to transfer the jump set of
\psi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
inside
B_{\rm bad}
.
First, we recall the main points from
[
26
]
, for any
Q_{i}=Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
: there are two lines
H^{n,+}_{i}
and
H^{n,-}_{i}
with normal
\nu_{\phi}(x_{i})
which lie above and below the middle line
H_{i}
containing the point
x_{i}
, also with normal
\nu_{\phi}(x_{i})
, such that

(7.13)

R^{n}_{i}\supset Q_{i}\cap\{y\colon|(y-x_{i})\cdot\nu_{\phi}(x_{i})|\leq 2\theta r_{i}\},

(7.14)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}L^{n}_{i}\big)\leq C\theta

for a universal constant
C>0
, where
R^{n}_{i}
denotes the rectangular subset that lies between
H^{n,+}_{i}
and
H^{n,-}_{i}
, and
L^{n}_{i}:=\partial R^{n}_{i}\setminus(H^{n,+}_{i}\cup H^{n,-}_{i})
denotes its lateral boundaries, cf.
[
26
, (2.10)–(2.11)]
and Figure
6
. Moreover, the construction provides a set of finite perimeter
P^{n}_{i}\subset Q_{i}=Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
such that

(7.15)

\Gamma^{n}_{i}:=\partial^{*}P^{n}_{i}\cap Q_{i}\subset R_{i}^{n}

satisfies

(7.16)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\big((\Gamma^{n}_{i}\cup L_{i}^{n})\setminus J_{v_{n}}\big)\leq C\theta r_{i}.

Note that, without restriction,
P^{n}_{i}
can be chosen such that both
P^{n}_{i}
and
Q_{i}\setminus P^{n}_{i}
are connected sets and hence
\Gamma^{n}_{i}
is a curve, see
[
32
, below (5.17)]
for the precise argument. We refer to
[
26
, Section 2]
or
[
32
, Section 5]
for more details and also refer to Figure
6
for an illustration of the construction. We mention that the construction is simplified compared to
[
32
,
26
]
as the sequence
(v_{n})_{n}
lies in
PC(U)
and thus
\nabla v_{n}\equiv 0
.

In
[
26
]
, the approximating functions
\phi_{n}
are defined by
\phi_{n}=\psi
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus B_{\rm bad}
and, in
B_{\rm bad}
, as
\phi_{n}=\psi
outside of
\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}R^{n}_{i}
and inside
\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}R^{n}_{i}
by reflection ensuring that

(7.17)

\displaystyle J_{\phi_{n}}\cap Q_{i}\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup L_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad\text{for all $Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}$}.

Here, we will use a variant of this construction, see
[
32
, Section 5]
, which is based on a cut-off construction and further ensures that, for a constant
C>0
,

(7.18)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq C\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\nabla\phi_{n}\in SBV(\Omega^{\prime};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{2{\times}2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\ \text{ with }\|\nabla(\nabla\phi_{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq C\theta^{-1}(\min\nolimits_{i}r_{i})^{-1}\|\nabla\psi\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla(\nabla\psi)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

and that, for a sufficiently small constant
c_{\theta}>0
depending on
\theta
,

(7.19)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm(i)}\ \bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n}\,\tilde{\subset}\,J_{\phi_{n}}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad{\rm(ii)}\;\ \mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\big\{x\in J_{\phi_{n}}\cap B_{\rm bad}\colon|[\phi_{n}](x)|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}\big)\leq C\theta.

We refer the reader to
[
32
, (5.16), (5.21), (5.22)]
.

For later purposes, we stress that we can assume without restriction that each connected component of
(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap Q_{i}
connects two different connected components of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
. (Here and in the sequel, we write
k
in place of
k(t)
.) In fact, if the start and endpoint of a component lie in the same connected component of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
, in the construction of
\phi_{n}
the curve
\Gamma^{n}_{i}
could be replaced by a curve that lies inside
K_{n}(t)
, i.e., on the boundary of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
, and such that (
7.16
) would still hold. For the same reason, it is not restrictive to assume that each connected components of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
meets at most two connected components of
(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap Q_{i}
as otherwise the curve could be replaced accordingly without affecting (
7.16
). Furthermore, we can assume that for each component
H
of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
, the set
\overline{H}
is not self-intersecting (cf., e.g., Figure
2
), since otherwise we could modify
K_{n}(t)
(and thus
J_{v_{n}}
) such that no self-intersections appear but (
7.16
) still holds.

For future notational purposes, we denote the two connected sets
P^{n}_{i}
and
Q_{i}\setminus P^{n}_{i}
and the restriction of the function on these sets by

(7.20)

P_{i}^{n,+}:=P^{n}_{i},\quad P_{i}^{n,-}:=Q_{i}\setminus P^{n}_{i},\quad\quad\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\phi_{i}^{n,\pm}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\phi_{n}|_{P_{i}^{n,\pm}}.

Q_{i}
H_{i}^{n,-}
H_{i}^{n,+}
L_{i}
K_{n}(t)

Q_{i}
H_{i}^{n,-}
H_{i}^{n,+}
L_{i}
J_{v_{n}}

Q_{i}
L_{i}
L_{i}
P_{i}^{n,+}
P_{i}^{n,-}
\Gamma_{i}^{n}

Figure 6
.
Construction of the continuous curve
\Gamma^{n}_{i}\subset R_{i}^{n}
from
J_{v_{n}}
, for
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
. In general, the curve
\Gamma_{i}^{n}
is a subset of
J_{v_{n}}
that is almost contained in
K_{n}
, which in turn is the boundary of the ‘modified crack set’
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
.
Note that the figure is only a schematic illustration: in fact, the actual thickness of
L_{i}^{n}
is much smaller.

Good squares

In the good squares we employ a similar construction to guarantee that the jump sets of the approximating functions are flat in
suitable squares of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}
and that also
\nabla\phi_{n}
lies in
W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus J_{\phi_{n}};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Such additional properties will be crucial in our following constructions.

First, we define the collection of squares

(7.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}:=\{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}&\colon|\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i}|>\theta^{-1}r_{i}\varepsilon_{n}\},\quad\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}:=\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large},\end{split}

and the sets

B_{\rm good}^{\rm large}:=\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}Q_{i},\quad\quad B_{\rm good}^{\rm small}:=\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}}Q_{i}\,.

(We notice that the sets defined above depend on
n
and
k
, but we neglect this dependence in the notation.)
We state a technical lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
7.5
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\theta>0
, let
E\subset\Omega^{\prime}
be induced by a union of triangles
\mathbf{T}_{E}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Omega^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(cf. (
2.10
)), and
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}
with
|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i}|\leq\theta^{-1}r_{i}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Then, there exists
\xi\in(-\theta r_{i},\theta r_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and a constant
\hat{C}>0
such that the set

A^{n}_{i}(\xi;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):=\big\{y\in Q_{i}\colon(y-x_{i})\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in(\xi-10^{8}\varepsilon_{n},\xi+10^{8}\varepsilon_{n})\big\}

fulfills

(7.22)

\displaystyle\#\big\{T\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}_{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\cap A^{n}_{i}(\xi;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\big\}\leq\frac{\hat{C}}{\theta^{2}}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

For
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
, we define

R^{n}_{i}=\{y\in Q_{i}\colon|(y-x)\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x)|\leq\theta r_{i}\},\quad\quad H^{n,\pm}_{i}=\{y\in Q_{i}\colon(y-x)\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x)=\pm\theta r_{i}\}.

(The sets are actually independent of
n
. Yet,
n
is added to have the same notation as for bad squares.) For any
Q_{i}=Q_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\in\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
, let
A^{n}_{i}:=A^{n}_{i}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{i}^{n};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack})
from Lemma
7.1
, and for

(7.23)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{i}^{n}:=\{y\in Q_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon(y-x_{i})\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x_{i})=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\},\quad P_{i}^{n,\pm}:=\{y\in Q_{i}\colon\pm(y-x_{i})\cdot\nu_{\psi}(x_{i})>\xi_{i}^{n}\}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

we apply the reflection lemma
[
35
, Lemma 3.4]
to the (restriction of the) function
\psi\in W^{2,\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus J_{\psi};\mathbb{R}^{2})
to
Q_{i}\setminus R_{i}^{n}
, which has Sobolev regularity by (
7.10
). The line
\{x_{2}=0\}
in the lemma corresponds to each of the two lines
H^{n,+}_{i}
and
H^{n,-}_{i}
. By this, we get
\phi_{i}^{n-}
,
\phi_{i}^{n,+}
of class
W^{2,\infty}
and set

(7.24)

\displaystyle\phi_{n}:=\phi_{i}^{n,-}\chi_{P_{i}^{n,-}}+\phi_{i}^{n,+}\chi_{P_{i}^{n,+}},

see also Figure
8
for an illustration.
By the above construction we get that

(7.25)

J_{\phi_{n}}\cap Q_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Gamma_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup L_{i}^{n},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq C\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\|\phi_{n}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(Q_{i}\setminus\Gamma_{i}^{n})}\leq C_{\psi,\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\psi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(Q_{i}\setminus J_{\psi})}\,,

where
L_{i}^{n}
denotes the lateral boundary also for
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
, i.e.,
L^{n}_{i}:=\partial R^{n}_{i}\setminus(H^{n,+}_{i}\cup H^{n,-}_{i})
, and where for shortness we denote by
C_{\psi,\theta}
the constant on the right-hand side of (
7.18
). Following again the strategy of
[
32
, Section 5]
as done for squares in
\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
, we get that for a small constant
c_{\theta}
only depending on
\theta
it holds

(7.26)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm(i)}\ \bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}}\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n}\,\tilde{\subset}\,J_{\phi_{n}}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad{\rm(ii)}\ \mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\big\{x\in J_{\phi_{n}}\cap B^{\rm small}_{\rm good}\colon|[\phi_{n}](x)|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}\big)\leq C\theta.

Definition of
\phi_{n}

Eventually, we define the function
\phi_{n}
on
B_{\rm bad}
and
B^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
as discussed above, and let

(7.27)

\phi_{n}=\psi\text{ on }\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(B_{\rm bad}\cup B^{\rm small}_{\rm good}).

Let us collect some of the main properties that we will use in the following. First, by the above construction it also holds for all
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
that

(7.28)

\phi_{n}=\psi\quad\text{in $Q_{i}\setminus R^{n}_{i}$}\,.

In view of (
7.18
) and (
7.25
), there is
C_{\psi}>0
depending on
\psi
and
C_{\psi,\theta}
additionally depending on
\theta
such that

(7.29)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus J_{\phi_{n}})}\leq C_{\psi},\quad\quad\quad\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus J_{\phi_{n}})}\leq C_{\psi,\theta}.

By (
7.19
) and (
7.26
), for a sufficiently small constant
c_{\theta}>0
depending on
\theta
, it holds that

(7.30)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm(i)}\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}}\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n}\,\tilde{\subset}\,J_{\phi_{n}}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad{\rm(ii)}\ \mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\big\{x\in J_{\phi_{n}}\cap(B_{\rm bad}\cup B^{\rm small}_{\rm good})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon|[\phi_{n}](x)|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}\big)\leq C\theta.

Moreover, by construction and recalling (
7.17
), (
7.25
) we find

(7.31)

J_{\phi_{n}}\cap{\rm int}(Q_{i})\,\tilde{\subset}\,\Gamma^{n}_{i},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad J_{\phi_{n}}\cap\partial Q_{i}\subset L_{i}^{n}\quad\text{for any }Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}

such that by (
7.9
) it holds

(7.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L^{n}_{i})\leq(1+2\theta)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap Q_{i})\quad\text{for all $Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}$ }.

7.2.
Definition of
\psi_{n}
and
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
, and proof of (
7.2
)

We now proceed with the definition of the sequence of displacements
(\psi_{n})_{n}
and the corresponding triangulations
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
. For
k=k(t)
such that
t\in[t_{n}^{k},t_{n}^{k+1})
, we consider
u_{n}(t)=u_{n}^{k}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{k}
and the corresponding triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
. In the following, we will use the identity
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(u_{n}^{k})=\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
. Recalling the partition of
\Omega^{\prime}
into
B_{\rm good}
,
B_{\rm bad}
, and
B_{\rm rest}
, we let

\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}:=\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})\colon T\cap B_{\rm bad}\neq\emptyset\big\},\quad\quad\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}:=\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})\colon T\cap(B_{\rm good}\cup B_{\rm bad})=\emptyset\big\},

and define the set
B^{n}_{\rm good}:=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}}T
. Note that
B^{n}_{\rm good}\cap B_{\rm bad}=\emptyset
and for
n
small enough we have
B_{\rm good}\subset B_{\rm good}^{n}
.
We will define the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
as

(7.33)

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})=\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k},

where
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
denotes a suitable triangulation of
B^{n}_{\rm good}
, which will be specified below. On every
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}
we choose
\psi_{n}
as the affine interpolation of
\psi
.

The main steps consist now in (a) defining
\psi_{n}
on the triangles of
\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
and in (b) defining
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
and the interpolation
\psi_{n}
on
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
.

(a) Bad squares

We define the sequence of displacements
(\psi_{n})_{n}
on triangles in
\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
. We fix
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
, and define the set of vertices

(7.34)

\mathcal{V}_{i,n}\coloneqq\big\{x\in\mathcal{V}(T)\colon\;T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k},\ T\cap Q_{i}\neq\emptyset\big\}\,,

where we denote the three vertices of the triangle
T
by
\mathcal{V}(T)
. We specify the value
\psi_{n}(x)
on each
x\in\mathcal{V}_{i,n}
in order to compute the piecewise affine interpolation on each triangle formed by the vertices
\mathcal{V}_{i,n}
. Recall that inside the closed set
Q_{i}
the jump
J_{\phi_{n}}
is included in the curve
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and the two lines
L^{n}_{i}
, see (
7.17
). For any vertex
x\in\mathcal{V}_{i,n}
with
x\notin\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
we simply set
\psi_{n}(x)\coloneqq\phi_{n}(x)
. Instead, if
x\in\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we proceed as follows. Since
\phi_{n}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus J_{\phi_{n}};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we know that
\phi^{n,+}_{i}(x)
and
\phi^{n,-}_{i}(x)
are well defined as boundary traces
for each
x\in\Gamma_{i}^{n}
, and as boundary traces also on
P_{i}^{n,\pm}\cap L^{n}_{i}
, respectively, cf. (
7.20
). For
x\in L^{n}_{i}\cap P_{i}^{n,\pm}
, we set
\psi_{n}(x)\coloneqq\phi_{i}^{n,\pm}(x)
. For

x\in\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t)
, we choose one of the values, e.g.,
\psi_{n}(x)\coloneqq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\phi^{n,+}_{i}(x)
. For
x\in\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cap K_{n}(t)
, there exists at least one triangle
T
with
x\in T
and
|T\cap\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|=0
because
K_{n}(t)=\partial\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
. We collect all such triangles in the set

(7.35)

\mathbf{T}_{\rm out}^{x}\coloneqq\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}^{x}\,\colon|T\cap\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}|=0\big\},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathbf{T}^{x}\coloneqq\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\in T\big\}\,.

Moreover, we let
N^{x}_{\rm out}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\coloneqq\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{x}_{\rm out}}T
be the neighborhood of
x
consisting of these surrounding triangles.
If
N^{x}_{\rm out}\,\tilde{\subset}\,P_{i}^{n,+}
, we set
\psi_{n}(x)\coloneqq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\phi^{n,+}_{i}(x)
(see the point
x_{1}
in Figure
7
). If
N^{x}_{\rm out}\,\tilde{\subset}\,P_{i}^{n,-}
, we set
\psi_{n}(x)\coloneqq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\phi^{n-}_{i}(x)
(see the point
x_{4}
in Figure
7
). Otherwise, we again choose an arbitrary value, e.g.,
\psi_{n}(x):=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\phi_{i}^{n,+}(x)
(see e.g. the point
x_{3}
in Figure
7
). For later purposes, we also define

(7.36)

\displaystyle N_{\rm out}(T)=\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{V}(T)}N_{\rm out}^{x}.

Now, we can define
\psi_{n}
on the union of the triangles in
\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
(and thus in particular on
B_{\rm bad}
)
by taking the piecewise affine interpolation on each triangle
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
.

x_{2}

Figure 7
.
Construction of
\psi_{n}
depending on the position of
N^{x}_{\rm out}
relative to
\Gamma_{i}^{n}
, for
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}
. In this case, we have e.g.
\psi_{n}(x_{1})=\phi_{i}^{n,+}(x_{1})
and
\psi_{n}(x_{4})=\phi_{i}^{n,-}(x_{4})
. For
x_{2}
and
x_{3}
we choose one of the two values.

(b) Good squares

We come to the definition of
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
and the corresponding interpolation
\psi_{n}
on
B_{\rm good}^{n}
. As the argument is local, without restriction we suppose that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}
consists of a single square
Q_{i}
. In the remaining part of this section, we will therefore drop the index
i
for simplicity, and write
Q
,
r
, as well as
A^{n}
(see before (
7.23
)).

For
Q\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}
, we set
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}=\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})\colon\,T\cap Q\neq\emptyset\}
, and we define
\psi_{n}
as the piecewise affine interpolation of
\psi
on each
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
.

Let us now come to the construction in the second case
Q\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
, i.e.,
|\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\cap Q|\leq\theta^{-1}r\varepsilon_{n}
.
As the underlying triangulation in
Q\setminus\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A^{n}
we will use
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
, whereas inside
A^{n}
we use the explicit construction of an optimal triangulation from
[
14
]
. However, in order to ensure that the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
is admissible, it has to fulfill the condition
\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
, see (
4.8
). Therefore, we need to treat the parts of
A^{n}
that are contained in the ‘pre-crack’
\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}
differently, which leads to a more involved construction, see Figure
8
.

We start by considering neighborhoods of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
in
Q
, by adding a buffer zone of order
10^{7}\varepsilon_{n}^{\prime}
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}=2\varepsilon_{n}\cos(\theta_{0})
around each ‘broken triangle’.
More precisely, we define the ‘enlarged crack sets’
N^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
and the corresponding collection of triangles as

(7.37)

\mathbf{T}^{\rm neigh}_{n,k}:=\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})\colon\dist\big(T,(\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\cap Q)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\leq 10^{7}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad N^{\rm crack}_{n,k}\coloneqq\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm neigh}_{n,k}}T\,.

By the definition of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
, we know that outside of
N^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
the triangles in
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
are part of a regular ‘background triangulation’
\mathbf{Z}_{n}
with size
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
. By the construction of
\Gamma_{i}^{n}
in
B_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
and due to Lemma
7.1
, the set
(J_{\phi_{n}}\setminus N^{\rm crack}_{n,k})\cap A^{n}
consists of at most
M:=\hat{C}\theta^{-2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1
straight segments
(S^{n}_{k})_{k=1}^{M}
. According to
[
14
, Proof of Proposition 4.1]
(see in particular
[
14
, (4.9) and Figure 4.10]
) there exists a triangulation
\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that, setting
\mathbf{\tilde{T}^{\prime}}_{n}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}\colon T\cap\bigcup_{k=1}^{M}S^{n}_{k}\neq\emptyset\}
, we have

(1)

J_{\phi_{n}}\cap\mathcal{V}(T)=\emptyset
for all
T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
.

(2)

All triangles
T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
with
\dist(T,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigcup_{k=1}^{M}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S^{n}_{k})\geq 5\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
are part of the ‘background’ triangulation
\mathbf{Z}_{n}
.

(3)

It holds

(7.38)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}^{\prime}}_{n}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{k=1}^{M}S^{n}_{k}\Big)\leq(1+2\theta)\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap Q\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,

where the second inequality in (
7.38
) follows from the construction of the segments
(S^{n}_{k})_{k}
and (
7.32
).

We now
define
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
as the set of triangles of the following type, see also Figure
8
for an illustration:

(i)

T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
with
T\cap Q\neq\emptyset
and
T\cap A^{n}=\emptyset
;

(ii)

T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm neigh}
with
T\cap A^{n}\neq\emptyset
;

(iii)

T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
with
T\cap A^{n}\neq\emptyset
and
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm neigh}
.

Recalling (
7.37
), we notice that this leads to a triangulation of
B^{n}_{\rm good}
. In fact, by construction all triangles
T\in\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
with
\dist(T,\bigcup_{k=1}^{N}S^{n}_{k})\geq 5\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
are part of
\mathbf{Z}_{n}
(see point (
2
) above)
and by the definition in (
4.4
) all triangles of
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
inside
\{x\in N^{\rm crack}_{n,k}\colon{\rm dist}(x,\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack})\geq 10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}^{\prime}\}
are part of
\mathbf{Z}_{n}
.

Eventually, on the triangles in
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
, we can define
\psi_{n}
as the interpolation of
\phi_{n}
on
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
. Note that also for
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
the interpolation is well defined because by construction none of the vertices of
T
belongs to
\bigcup_{k}S^{n}_{k}
(see point (
1
) above).

(c) Conclusion

We have now concluded the definition of the function
\psi_{n}
and of the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
. As by construction
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
fulfills
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
(actually, it even holds
\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\subset\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
), we have
\psi_{n}\in\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{k}(\Omega^{\prime})
, see before (
4.8
). For later purposes, we note that

(7.39)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{n}|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa\quad\quad\text{for all $T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}$}.

Indeed, since
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
, by (
4.4
) we know that
\varepsilon_{n}|e(u_{n}^{j})_{T}|^{2}<\kappa
and
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u_{n}^{j}))<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
j\leq k
such that
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{j})
.
As
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})
, we can conclude that either
\varepsilon_{n}|e(\psi_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa
or
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(\psi_{n}))\geq 10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. If
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
we have, by the construction of
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
, that
T\in\tilde{\mathbf{T}}_{n}
and thus by (
2
) it holds
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(\psi_{n}))<10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}
.
However, if
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
, we have by the above argument that
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(u_{n}^{k}))<10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}
, which leads to
\dist(T,\mathbf{Z}_{n}(\psi_{n}))<10^{6}\varepsilon_{n}
. In both cases, this yields
\varepsilon_{n}|e(\psi_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa
, i.e., (
7.39
) holds.

Since we assumed that
\partial_{D}\Omega\cap J_{\psi}=\emptyset
, we can also suppose that for
n
large enough we have
(B_{\rm bad}\cup B^{n}_{\rm good})\cap\partial_{D}\Omega=\emptyset
. Therefore, we obtain
\psi_{n}=g(t)_{\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})}
on all
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
with
T\cap\overline{\Omega}=\emptyset
, which yields
\psi_{n}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{k}(g(t))
, see the definition before (
7.2
). Moreover, by the regularity of
\psi\in\mathcal{W}(\Omega^{\prime})
we have that
\psi_{n}\to\psi
in measure on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(B_{\rm good}\cup B_{\rm bad})
. Therefore, by the first estimate in (
7.11
) it follows that, for all
\delta>0
,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}|\{|\psi_{n}-\psi|>\delta\}|\leq|(B_{\rm good}\cup B_{\rm bad})|\leq\theta.

We thus have validated that (
7.2
) holds for
\psi_{n}
.

L_{i}^{n}
J_{\psi}
J_{\phi_{n}}
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}

Q
