N_{n,k}^{\rm crack}

Figure 8
.
Construction of
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
in
Q\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
. First, we transfer the jump from
J_{\psi}
to the flat set
J_{\phi_{n}}
, whose vertical coordinate is determined by Lemma
7.1
. In the golden neighborhoods
N_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
of connected components of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
(which are actually
of size much larger than the triangles), we keep the triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
, and in the red region we use the triangulation
\mathbf{\tilde{T}}_{n}
given by
[
14
]
.

7.3.
Proof of (
7.3
): Stability estimate of the crack part

We now proceed with the proof of (
7.3
). Again let
t\in[0,T]
be given and, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, choose
k=k(t)
such that
t\in[t^{k}_{n},t_{n}^{k+1})
. Let
(\psi_{n})_{n}
be the sequence defined above with the associated triangulation
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
. Note that by definition we have

(7.40)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n};t)-\mathcal{E}_{n}^{\rm crack}(u_{n}(t);t)\leq\sum_{T\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

We split
\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}(\psi_{n})
into three different parts, namely
\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})
,
\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,b}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})
, and
\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,r}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})
, which corresponds to the intersection of
\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}(\psi_{n})
with
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good}_{n,k}
,
\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
, and
\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}
, respectively, see (
7.33
).

Bad squares

We start with the bad squares and aim at estimating

(7.41)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,b}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C\theta

for a universal constant
C>0
. This will rely on the following two lemmas whose proofs will be deferred to Section
7.5
below. For their formulation, we recall the definition of the neighborhood
N_{\rm out}(T)
in (
7.36
) and of the sets
P_{i}^{n,\pm}
in (
7.20
), see also Figure
7
. We then introduce the set of triangles, where
N_{\rm out}(T)
is contained in one of the sets
P_{i}^{n,+}
or
P_{i}^{n,-}
, namely

(7.42)

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}=\Big\{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}\colon\ \ N_{\rm out}(T)\subset\overline{P_{i}^{n,+}}\text{ or }N_{\rm out}(T)\subset\overline{P_{i}^{n,-}}\text{ for some }Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\Big\}.

We also recall the definition of
L^{n}_{i}
and
\Gamma_{i}^{n}
in (
7.14
) and (
7.15
), respectively.

Lemma 7.2
.

Consider
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n,k}
satisfying one of the conditions

(a)

T\cap(L^{n}_{i}\cup\Gamma^{i}_{n})=\emptyset
,

(b)

T\cap L^{n}_{i}=\emptyset
and
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
.

Then, it holds
|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq C
for some
C
only depending on
\psi
, and in particular, for
n
large enough, we have by (
7.39
) that
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

For
n
large enough, the curves
\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
satisfy the properties

(7.43)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}\#\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon\ \emptyset\neq T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset K_{n}(t),\ T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}\leq\frac{C\,\theta}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

(7.44)

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\#\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon\,(\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap T\neq\emptyset\text{ or }L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap T\neq\emptyset\big\}\leq\frac{C\,\theta}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

With these auxiliary results we can prove (
7.41
).
In view of (
7.43
), (
7.44
), and the fact that
|T|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,b}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})
(recall (
4.1
)), it is enough to confirm that each
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,b}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
lies in the collection of triangles estimated in (
7.43
) or (
7.44
). Indeed, all triangles not lying in these collections either satisfy (a) or (b) of Lemma
7.2
or fulfill
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
. In both cases, it holds
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,b}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
for
n
large enough, and thus we obtain a contradiction.

Good squares

For the good squares, our goal is to prove

(7.45)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}\big((J_{\psi}\setminus K(t))\cap B_{\rm good}\big)+C\theta\,.

We define the ‘cracked triangles’ in
Q_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}
according to
\psi_{n}
by

(7.46)

\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})=\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap(J_{\phi_{n}}\cap Q_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\big\}\,.

We state an important property whose proof is again deferred to Section
7.5
.

Lemma 7.4
.

It holds that

(7.47)

\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}\subset\bigcup_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}}\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})\,.

Recalling the distinction in (
7.21
), we first address squares
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}
. In fact, we want to prove that

(7.48)

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq C\theta.

By definition we find that for each
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}
we have
r_{i}<\theta\,\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}|\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i}|
such that the energy bound in Corollary
6.4
implies
\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}r_{i}\leq C\theta\,
. By (
7.9
) we find

(7.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{i})\leq Cr_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap B_{\rm good}^{\rm large})\leq C\theta.

Then, by construction and the regularity of
J_{\psi}
we have
\#\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})\leq Cr_{i}/\varepsilon_{n}
for all
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}
(see (
7.27
)), i.e., by (
4.1
) we get

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}C\varepsilon_{n}\#\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})\leq\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm large}}Cr_{i}\leq C\theta\,,

and hence (
7.48
).
We now proceed with
\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
. Recall that
J_{\phi_{n}}\cap Q_{i}\subset\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L^{n}_{i}
, see (
7.23
)–(
7.25
). Recalling also the construction of
\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
in
Q_{i}
, in particular the definition of
\mathbf{\tilde{T}^{\prime}}_{n,i}
for each
Q_{i}
, we have

(7.50)

\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})\subset\mathbf{\tilde{T}^{\prime}}_{n,i}\cup\big\{T\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}_{n,k,i}^{\rm neigh}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap A_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\big\}\cup\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}\,.

Here,

\mathbf{T}_{n,k,i}^{\rm neigh}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{T}^{\prime}}_{n,i}
are defined as in (
7.37
) and above (
7.38
), where we also include the subscript
i
in the notation since the objects are related to
Q_{i}
. We estimate the energetic contribution for each of the three families of triangles in (
7.50
):
for the first family, we can make use of (
7.38
) and we obtain, for any
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
,

(7.51)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\tilde{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}_{n,i}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq(1+2\theta)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i})\,.

For the second family, by definition of
\mathbf{T}^{\rm neigh}_{n,k,i}
in (
7.37
) and Lemma
7.1
, we get

(7.52)

\#\big\{T\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{T}_{n,k,i}^{\rm neigh}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap A_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\big\}\leq C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\#\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\big\}\leq C\frac{\hat{C}}{\theta^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

where the
C
depends on the constant
10^{7}
. For the third family, since
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq Cr_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta
, we deduce

(7.53)

\#\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{i}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}\leq C\frac{\theta r_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

Therefore, in view of the fact that
|T|\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
by (
4.1
), collecting (
7.51
)–(
7.53
) and using (
7.9
) we get that for each
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
and for
n
large enough it holds

\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\frac{\hat{C}}{\theta^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\theta r_{i}.

From Lemma
7.4
and the fact that
\sum_{i}r_{i}\leq C

we can conclude that

(7.54)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}}\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap Q_{i})+C\theta.

In view of (
7.8
) and using again that
\sum_{i}r_{i}\leq C
, this implies (
7.45
).

The remaining part

Finally, we want to show that

(7.55)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,r}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq C\theta\,.

Recall that for all
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}
we have
\psi_{n}=\psi
on the vertices of
T
, see below (
7.33
). In particular, for all triangles

T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}
with
T\cap J_{\psi}=\emptyset
we have
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|e(\psi)_{T}|\leq|\nabla\psi_{T}|\leq C
and hence,
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,r}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
for
n
large enough by (
7.39
). Therefore, it suffices to consider all
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}
with
T\cap J_{\psi}\neq\emptyset
. Due to (
7.12
) and the regularity of
J_{\psi}
, we obtain

(7.56)

\#\{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}\colon T\cap J_{\psi}\neq\emptyset\}\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\cap B_{\rm rest})\leq C\frac{\theta}{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.

Altogether, we obtain
\#(\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,r}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k})\leq C\frac{\theta}{\varepsilon_{n}}
. This along with (
4.1
) yields (
7.55
).

Conclusion

By putting together (
7.41
), (
7.45
), and (
7.55
) we conclude

(7.57)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k-1}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}}\frac{|T|}{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\sin(\theta_{0})\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\psi}\setminus K(t))+C\theta\,.

By (
7.40
) this finally validates (
7.3
).

7.4.
Proof of (
7.4
): Stability estimate for elastic part

Finally, we come to the proof of (
7.4
). Recall the definition of approximating functions
\phi_{n}
from the paragraph ‘Jump transfer’ in Section
7.1
. We define the collection of triangles which do not intersect the jump by

\mathbf{D}_{n}(\phi_{n})\coloneqq\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T\cap J_{\phi_{n}}=\emptyset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\},\quad\quad D_{n}(\phi_{n})\coloneqq\bigcup_{T\in\mathbf{D}_{n}(\phi_{n})}T\,.

Next, we introduce the set of triangles
T
outside
\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})
where
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|^{2}
is potentially not uniformly controlled. To this end, we let

G_{n,k}(\psi_{n}):=\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{G}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})}T,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{n,k}(\psi_{n}):=\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n})\neq\emptyset\text{ for }Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{ or }T\notin\mathbf{D}_{n}(\phi_{n})\big\}.\end{split}

We split the elastic energy
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{\rm elast}(\psi_{n},t)
into the two parts

(7.58)

\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})}|e(\psi_{n})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x=\int_{\Omega\setminus(G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\cup\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n}))}|e(\psi_{n})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})}|e(\psi_{n})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\,.

In order to estimate the second term, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.5
.

For
\varepsilon_{n}
small enough, it holds that

(7.59)

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}}\#\,\big(\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap\Gamma_{i}^{n}\neq\emptyset\}\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})\big)\leq C\theta\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}.

We defer the proof to Section
7.5
. In view of (
7.44
) and (
7.53
), we have

(7.60)

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}}\#\,\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon T\cap L_{i}^{n}\neq\emptyset\}\leq C\theta\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}.

Let
\mathbf{T}^{\rm good,large}_{n,k}=\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})\colon\,T\cap B_{\rm good}^{\rm large}\neq\emptyset\}
. From (
7.27
), (
7.49
), and the argument in (
7.56
) we deduce

(7.61)

\#\big((\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm good,large}_{n,k})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{D}_{n}(\phi_{n})\big)\leq C\theta\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}\,.

By Lemma
7.5
, (
7.60
), (
7.61
), and (
7.31
), recalling
|T|\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
for any
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})
by (
4.1
) and using
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|^{2}\leq\kappa\color[rgb]{0,0,0}/\varepsilon_{n}
for all
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})
, we obtain

(7.62)

|G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})|\leq C\theta\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})}|e(\psi_{n})|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq C\theta\,.

Therefore, we are left to estimate the elastic energy contribution on
\Omega\setminus(G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\cup\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n}))
. We first show that for each
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\setminus(\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})\cup\mathcal{G}_{n,k}(\psi_{n}))
, the function
\phi_{n}|_{T}
has
W^{2,\infty}
-regularity. Indeed, if
T
intersects
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}^{\rm small}
with
T\cap(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n})=\emptyset
and
T\subset Q_{i}
, it follows that either
T\subset{P_{i}^{n,-}}
or
T\subset{P_{i}^{n,+}}
(see (
7.20
) and (
7.23
)), and thus
\phi_{n}
coincides with
\phi_{i}^{n,-}
or
\phi_{i}^{n,+}
in
T
, respectively (see (
7.20
) and (
7.24
)). If
T\cap(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\cup L_{i}^{n})=\emptyset
and
T\setminus Q_{i}\neq\emptyset
, then
\phi_{n}
coincides with
\psi
in
T
by (
7.28
). If
T\in(\mathbf{T}^{\rm rest}_{n,k}\cup\mathbf{T}^{\rm good,large}_{n,k})\cap\mathbf{D}_{n}(\phi_{n})
, we have that
\phi_{n}=\psi
on
T
, see (
7.27
).

In all cases, we get
\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(T)}\leq C_{\psi,\theta}
by (
7.29
). Since on all such triangles
\psi_{n}
is the piecewise affine interpolation of
\phi_{n}
, using (
4.1
) we derive

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e(\psi_{n})-e(\phi_{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(T)}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla\psi_{n}-\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(T)}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi,\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\varepsilon_{n}\quad\ \ \text{for all $T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\setminus(\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})\cup\mathcal{G}_{n,k}(\psi_{n}))$}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

By summing over all triangles this gives

(7.63)

\int_{\Omega\setminus(G_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\cup\Omega_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n}))}|e(\psi_{n})-e(\phi_{n})|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq|\Omega|\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C^{2}_{\psi,\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\varepsilon_{n}^{2}.

By the bound on
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla\phi_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
in (
7.29
), the first property in (
7.11
), and (
7.27
) we further find

\int_{\Omega}|e(\phi_{n})|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{\Omega}|e(\psi)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C\theta.

This and (
7.63
) control the limsup of the first integral on the right-hand side of (
7.58
). Therefore, in view of (
7.62
), the proof of (
7.4
) is concluded.

7.5.
Proof of lemmas

Finally, we prove the lemmas used in the previous subsections.

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

We consider
\xi_{j}\coloneqq(-\theta r_{i}+2j\cdot 10^{8}\varepsilon_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for
j=0,\ldots,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{N}:=\lceil\frac{\theta r_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{10^{8}\varepsilon_{n}}\rceil
and observe that for
n
large enough

\bigcup_{j=0}^{{N}}A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j})\supset R^{n}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i}|\geq\sum_{j=1}^{N-2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j})|,

denoting, here and below,
A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j};E)
by
A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j})
. Therefore, we find
J\in\{2,\ldots,N-3\}
such that

{\sum_{j=J-1}^{J+1}|E\cap A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j})|\leq\tilde{C}N^{-1}|E\cap Q_{i}|\leq\tilde{C}\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/\theta^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

for some universal
\tilde{C}>0
, where we used the assumption that
|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i}|\leq\theta^{-1}r_{i}\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. As
|T|\geq c\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
for
c
only depending on
\theta_{0}
, we obtain

\#\Big\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\subset\bigcup_{j=J-1}^{J+1}A^{n}_{i}(\xi_{j})\ \Big\}\leq\tilde{C}/\theta^{2}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In view of (
4.1
), this shows the statement for
\xi=\xi_{J}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We first consider the case that
T\cap(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\emptyset
. As it holds
\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus J_{\phi_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq C_{\psi}
by (
7.29
), we find by (
7.31
) that

|\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(x^{\prime})|\leq\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}(T)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|x-x^{\prime}|

for vertices
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(T)
. In particular, as
\psi_{n}(x)=\phi_{n}(x)
and
\psi_{n}(x^{\prime})=\phi_{n}(x^{\prime})
, this implies

(7.64)

|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}(x-x^{\prime})|=|\psi_{n}(x)-\psi_{n}(x^{\prime})|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|x-x^{\prime}|\,.

Since this holds true along all three edges of
T
, we deduce that also
|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq C
for some
C>0
, as desired.

Now, we assume that
T\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\emptyset
but
T\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
and
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. By definition of
\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}
in (
7.42
), we find that either
N_{\rm out}(T)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,P^{n,+}_{i}
or
N_{\rm out}(T)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\tilde{\subset}\,P_{i}^{n,-}
and

N_{\rm out}(T)\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{n,\pm}_{i}
if and only if
T\,\tilde{\subset}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{n,\pm}_{i}
, i.e.,
\psi_{n}
is the piecewise affine interpolation of
\phi_{i}^{n,\pm}
. Arguing as above, we deduce that
|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq C
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

We start with the proof of (
7.43
). Consider
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n}
such that
\emptyset\neq T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset K_{n}(t)
and
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. As
\partial T\cap\partial\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\neq\emptyset
and
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}
, we first notice that, in view of Remark
3.5
, we have
|T\cap\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}|=0
.
Then we may assume that
T\subset N_{\rm out}^{x}
for all
x\in\mathcal{V}(T)
, hence
N_{\rm out}(T)
is a connected set. As
\emptyset\neq T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\subset K_{n}(t)
, we get
N_{\rm out}(T)\cap\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
. However, since
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}
, we also have
N_{\rm out}(T)\cap P^{n,+}_{i}\neq\emptyset
and at the same time
N_{\rm out}(T)\cap P^{n,-}_{i}\neq\emptyset
. This implies that
T
is either close to the intersection of a component of
\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t)
and a component of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
or it is close to the intersection of two different components in
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
(see, e.g.,
x_{2}
in Figure
7
or
x_{3}
in this picture, respectively).

Recall that each connected components of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
meets at most two connected components of
\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t)
, see the discussion before (
7.20
). For the same reason, we can assume that for each connected component
H
of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
, the set
H\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}
intersects at most two other components in
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
.
Summarizing, the number of triangles
T
of the above form is controlled (up to a multiplicative constant) by the total number of connected components
\mathcal{C}(\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod})
. By (
2.14
) and Corollary
6.4
we thus obtain

(7.65)

\#\big\{T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm bad}_{n}\colon\ \emptyset\neq T\cap\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset K_{n}(t),\ T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\rm side}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\mathbf{T}_{n,k}^{\rm crack}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}\leq C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Since
\eta_{n}<\theta
for
n
large enough, we conclude the proof of (
7.43
).

We now come to the proof of (
7.44
). The triangles
T
with
L^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap T\neq\emptyset
can be controlled exactly as in (
7.53
). By
(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})_{l}
we denote the connected components of
(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap Q_{i}
, and recall that we assumed without restriction that
\Gamma^{n}_{i,l}
connects two different connected components of
\Omega_{n,k}^{\rm mod}
, see the discussion before (
7.20
). Note that
K_{n}(t)
is the boundary of a union of triangles in
\mathbf{T}_{n}(u_{n}^{k})
, and thus different connected components of
K_{n}(t)
have distance at least
c\varepsilon_{n}
for some
c>0
only depending on
\theta_{0}
. Therefore, each
\Gamma^{n}_{i,l}
fulfills
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\geq c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{n}
. The key point consists in showing

(7.66)

\#\{T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T\cap\Gamma_{i,l}^{n}\neq\emptyset\}\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{i,l}^{n}).

Once this is shown, by summing over all components
(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})_{l}
, we get

(7.67)

\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}\#\{T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\colon\,(\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap T\neq\emptyset\}\leq\sum_{Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}}\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{1}\big((\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap Q_{i}\big)\,.

By using (
7.6
)
and (
7.16
) we have
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}((\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus K_{n}(t))\cap B_{\rm bad})\leq C\theta
, which concludes the proof of (
7.44
).

To prove (
7.66
), we consider the
\omega(\varepsilon_{n})
-neighborhood of
\Gamma^{n}_{i,l}
, i.e.,
N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l}):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\colon{\rm dist}(x,\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\leq\omega(\varepsilon_{n})\}\,
.
As
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\geq c\varepsilon_{n}
, an elementary geometric argument yields the existence of a universal constant
C>0
such that

\big|N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\big|\leq C\,\omega(\varepsilon_{n})\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\leq C\varepsilon_{n}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\,,

where we used (
4.1
). Since
|T|\geq c\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
, we hence obtain

\#\big\{T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\,\Gamma^{n}_{i,l}\cap T\neq\emptyset\big\}\leq\#\big\{T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\,T\subset N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\big\}\leq C\frac{\big|N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\big|}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{n}_{i,l})\,,

which validates (
7.66
). This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

We argue by contradiction and assume that there exists
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
with
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})
for all
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm good}
. In view of (
7.39
), we have
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa/\varepsilon_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for
T\in\mathbf{T}^{\rm cr,g}_{n,k}(\psi_{n})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathbf{T}^{\rm crack}_{n,k}
. Since
T\notin\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{n}(\psi_{n},Q_{i})
, i.e.,
T\cap(J_{\phi_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{i})=\emptyset
, we can deduce
\|\phi_{n}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(T)}\leq C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by (
7.29
). Now, consider vertices
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(T)
and recall that by definition
\psi_{n}(x)=\phi_{n}(x)
and
\psi_{n}(x^{\prime})=\phi_{n}(x^{\prime})
. We obtain

(7.68)

|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}(x-x^{\prime})|=|\psi_{n}(x)-\psi_{n}(x^{\prime})|=|\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(x^{\prime})|\leq\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(T)}|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{n}\,.

Since this holds along all three edges of
T
, there exists a constant
C>0
, such that
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq|(\nabla\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq{C}
, which for large
n\in\mathbb{N}
contradicts the fact that
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|^{2}\geq\kappa/\varepsilon_{n}
. Hence, (
7.47
) holds.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

We follow the argumentation in
[
32
, Lemma 5.2]
, which relies on the fact that the measure of jump points with small jump height is small, cf. (
7.30
). Fix
T\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(\psi_{n})\setminus\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})
such that
T\cap\Gamma_{i}^{n}\neq\emptyset
, for some
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm bad}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\rm small}_{\rm good}
. By (
7.30
)(i) we then get that also
J_{\phi_{n}}\cap T\neq\emptyset
. Let
\tilde{x}\in T\cap J_{\phi_{n}}
and choose
x\in T\cap\overline{P_{i}^{n,+}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
x^{\prime}\in T\cap\overline{P_{i}^{n,-}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(recall (
7.20
) and (
7.24
)) in such a way that
\tilde{x}
lies on the segment between
x
and
x^{\prime}
, and
|x-x^{\prime}|\geq c\varepsilon_{n}
. (If
T\subset\overline{P_{i}^{n,+}}
, we choose
x^{\prime}=\tilde{x}
, and if
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T\subset\overline{P_{i}^{n,-}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we choose
x=\tilde{x}
. In the following, we assume that
\tilde{x}\neq x,x^{\prime}
. In the other case, the argument is similar using the one-sided traces of
\phi_{n}
at
\tilde{x}
.) From
T\notin\mathbf{T}_{n,k-1}^{\rm crack}(\psi_{n})
, we have that
|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|\leq\sqrt{\kappa/\varepsilon_{n}}
. Since
\phi_{n}
is Lipschitz on
Q_{i}\setminus\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma^{n}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
7.29
) and (
7.31
)), we get
|\phi_{n}(x)-\psi_{n}(x)|,|\phi_{n}(x^{\prime})-\psi_{n}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\psi}\varepsilon_{n}+C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by (
4.1
), and therefore

\Big\langle\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(x^{\prime}),\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle\leq\Big\langle\psi_{n}(x)-\psi_{n}(x^{\prime}),\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle+C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\Big\langle e(\psi_{n})_{T}\cdot(x-x^{\prime}),\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle+C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

By this fact and
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\omega(\varepsilon_{n})\leq C\varepsilon_{n}
(see (
4.1
)), we hence can estimate

(7.69)

\Big\langle\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(x^{\prime}),\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle\leq|e(\psi_{n})_{T}|\,\varepsilon_{n}+C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

On the other hand, by the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus we have that

(7.70)

\Big\langle\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(x^{\prime}),\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle=\int_{0}^{1}\Big\langle\nabla\phi_{n}(x+s(x^{\prime}-x))\cdot(x^{\prime}-x),\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle\,{\rm d}s+\Big\langle[\phi_{n}(\tilde{x})],\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle.

By (
7.29
) and
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq C\varepsilon_{n}
, the first term on the right-hand side is of order
\varepsilon_{n}
. Putting together (
7.69
) and (
7.70
) we thus obtain

\Big\langle[\phi_{n}(\tilde{x})],\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\Big\rangle\leq C\sqrt{\,\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\varepsilon_{n}\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Letting
\nu_{1}\coloneqq\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{|x^{\prime}-x|}
this means
\langle[\phi_{n}(\tilde{x})],\nu_{1}\rangle\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}
. Now we can repeat the procedure for different
\hat{x}
and
\hat{x}^{\prime}
, where the segment between
\hat{x}
and
\hat{x}^{\prime}
intersects
J_{\phi_{n}}
also exactly at
\tilde{x}
and
\nu_{2}\coloneqq\frac{\hat{x}-\hat{x}^{\prime}}{|\hat{x}-\hat{x}^{\prime}|}
satisfies
\langle\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\rangle\geq c
for a universal
c>0
. We then have
\langle[\phi_{n}(\tilde{x})],\nu_{i}\rangle\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}
for
i=1,2
, which leads to
|[\phi_{n}(\tilde{x})]|\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}
.

We consider

N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\colon{\rm dist}(x,T\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\varepsilon_{n}\}\,,
where we use
\|\nabla\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T)\setminus J_{\phi_{n}})}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
to find

|[\phi_{n}(z)]|\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{n}\quad\text{for all}\;z\in J_{\phi_{n}}\cap N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T)\,.

(In fact,
\tilde{x}
and each
z
can be connected by two different curves on different components of
N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T)\setminus\overline{J_{\phi_{n}}}
of length
\sim\varepsilon_{n}
.) As
\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\phi_{n}}\cap N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T))
is at least
\varepsilon_{n}
, we get for
\varepsilon_{n}
small enough depending on
\theta

{\mathcal{H}^{1}\big(\{z\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in J_{\phi_{n}}\cap N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T)\colon|[\phi_{n}](z\color[rgb]{0,0,0})|\leq c_{\theta}\}\big)=\mathcal{H}^{1}(J_{\phi_{n}}\cap N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T))\geq\varepsilon_{n}.}

Summing over all
T
of the form described above, observing that each neighborhood
N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T)
intersects only a bounded number of neighborhoods
N_{\varepsilon_{n}}(T^{\prime})
,
T^{\prime}\neq T
, and using (
7.30
) we conclude that (
7.59
) holds.
∎
